To ensure inclusivity, the country has been improving accessibility for travelers with different needs. From accessible transportation and accommodations to government initiatives, here’s a complete guide on accessibility in India for tourists. 1. Government Initiatives for Accessible Tourism The Government of India has launched several programs to promote barrier-free travel. Sugamya Bharat Abhiyan (Accessible India Campaign): Aims to make public spaces, transportation, and tourist sites more accessible. Incredible India Campaign: Highlights inclusive tourism and promotes destinations that accommodate people with disabilities. UNESCO Collaboration: Some heritage sites have been upgraded with ramps, wheelchairs, and audio guides. 2. Accessible Transportation Traveling across India is becoming easier with improved transport facilities: Air Travel: Major airports like Delhi, Mumbai, Bengaluru, and Hyderabad have wheelchair-friendly terminals, special assistance counters, and priority boarding. Railways: Indian Railways offers wheelchair booking, accessible toilets in select coaches, and escort services at major stations. Road Travel: Many private cab services (like Uber Assist and wheelchair taxis in metros) cater to travelers with mobility needs. Metro Systems: Cities like Delhi, Bengaluru, and Hyderabad have modern metro services equipped with elevators, ramps, tactile flooring, and priority seating. 3. Accessible Accommodation Hotels and resorts across India are increasingly designed to be inclusive: International Chains (Marriott, Taj, Hyatt, ITC): Offer wheelchair-accessible rooms, ramps, elevators, and bathrooms with grab bars. Budget & Mid-Range Hotels: Growing numbers now include basic accessibility features. Online Booking Filters: Websites like Booking.com and Airbnb let tourists filter properties with accessibility features. 4. Accessible Tourist Attractions Many popular attractions are upgrading to welcome all travelers: Taj Mahal (Agra): Wheelchairs available, ramps at entrances, battery-operated vehicles from parking areas. Qutub Minar (Delhi): Improved pathways and ramps for mobility. Kerala Backwaters: Accessible houseboats designed for tourists with limited mobility. Jaipur Monuments: Amber Fort and City Palace have partial accessibility features with support services. Beaches (Goa, Chennai): Some beaches offer wheelchair access with support from NGOs. 5. Technology & Apps for Accessible Travel Digital platforms simplify travel planning for accessible tourism in India: MakeMyTrip & Yatra: Allow filtering for accessible hotels. Google Maps Accessibility Feature: Shows wheelchair-friendly routes and entrances. Assistive Apps: “EzyMov” and “Mobility India” provide on-demand wheelchair taxi services in some cities. 6. Challenges in Accessibility Despite improvements, challenges remain: Many heritage sites are partially accessible due to historical architecture. Rural areas still lack infrastructure for tourists with disabilities . Public transport outside major cities may not always support accessibility needs. 7. Tips for Accessible Travel in India Plan trips around metros and tier-1 cities for better infrastructure. Pre-book wheelchair assistance for flights and trains . Stay at international or top-rated hotels for guaranteed accessibility features. Use apps and online forums for reviews by other travelers with accessibility needs. Consider guided tours specializing in accessible tourism for a smooth experience. 8. Conclusion Accessibility in India is steadily improving, with major airports, hotels, and tourist attractions offering better facilities for travelers with different needs. While challenges remain in rural and historic areas, urban destinations are increasingly inclusive. With proper planning, tourists can enjoy India’s diverse culture, heritage, and natural beauty in a more comfortable and accessible way. Read more.","content":"Travelling To India With Disabilities - What To Know Before You Go Planning Your Trip India Travel Introduction India Travel Packing List Where To Go When To Go Vaccinations Visas & Immigration Travelling With Children Travellers With Disabilities On The Ground Local Customs & Culture Getting Around India Train Travel Guide Eating Sleeping Shopping Festivals & Events Health & Safety Coronavirus Air Pollution Delhi Eating Hydration Parasites Mosquitoes Women Travellers Are malarial medicines worth it? Altitude Sickness Police, Drugs & The Law Scammers & Tricksters Vaccinations Personal Hygiene Attractions Temples & Sightseeing Beaches National Parks Tiger Safaris Backwaters Yoga & Meditation Search Travellers With Disabilities Travelling to India with disabilities? What to know before you go… There’s very little infrastructure for those people travelling to India with disabilities. Moving through the chaos on India’s streets and roads is frustrating enough for the sprightliest of travellers, let alone someone with mobility issues. Access to buildings will mostly be difficult, but on the plus side there’ll usually be some person nearby to provide a helping hand. Finding a clean public toilet in India would be a bit like finding water in the desert, so the idea of finding a clean public toilet with easy access might be compared to discovering water on the moon! On the other hand it’s entirely possible for a traveller with disabilities to enjoy an adventure in India, provided you’re willing to pay for private car hire and mid-luxury guesthouses along the way. Facilities are improving all the time and many of the key tourist sites now have ramps for chair users (always check before you go). Several airports (particularly those with international flights) have also received a huge refurbishment in recent years and are now well equipped with easy access toilets and other facilities for travellers with disabilities. Travelling in India with a disability may feel restricting at times compared to other tourist destinations and I would strongly advise taking an able-bodied companion along with you, who can scout out any hindrances and facilitate the crossing of any unforeseen hurdles along the way. I tend to advise people not to make any concrete plans when travelling in India, but if you’re travelling with mobility issues, then I would say it’s much better to have a pre-determined programme in place before you travel. Try to communicate with hotels and tour operators in advance to determine that your proposed itinerary is possible based upon your own requirements. There are a lot less tour operators offering bespoke packages for accessible tourism in India than one would wish for. I suppose this is indicative of the fact that India still remains to be a challenging travel destination for the disabled traveller and I hope that more options become available in the near future as India steps up to become a new world power. For the time being I would highly recommend booking a tour with these guys… https://www.enableholidays.com/India Enjoyed reading this post? please share it Police, Drugs & The Law Tiger Safaris © 2025 India Travel Dreams. Accessible India Tours are specially designed travel packages for international tourists with mobility issues, including wheelchair users and seniors. They ensure barrier-free travel across India with adapted transport, hotels, and relaxed itineraries. Q: Are these tours suitable for wheelchair users? Yes, all our wheelchair accessible India tour packages include modified vehicles, ramps at major sites, and disabled-friendly hotels. Q: Do you provide accessible Golden Triangle tours? Absolutely. Our Golden Triangle wheelchair tour covers Delhi, Agra, and Jaipur with accessible stays and guided visits to monuments like the Taj Mahal and Amber Fort (view points only if ramps are limited). Q: Which cities in India are best for disabled travelers? Delhi, Agra, Jaipur, Udaipur, and Jodhpur are the most adapted cities with better infrastructure and hotel options for travelers with disabilities. Q: What kind of hotels are included in these tours? We select disabled-friendly hotels in India with wheelchair access, lift availability, grab bars in bathrooms, and staff trained in assisting guests with mobility needs. Q: Are female guides available for accessible tours? Yes, we can provide female English-speaking guides on request, especially helpful for solo women or elderly travelers. Q: Can these tours be customized based on personal limitations? Definitely. All our accessible tour packages are fully customizable to suit your pace, needs, and interests. Q: Do you offer senior citizen-friendly tours of India? Yes, many of our clients are 60+ travelers. We design slow-paced, senior-friendly India tours with shorter sightseeing hours and relaxing activities. Q: Are accessible vehicles included in the tour? Yes, we provide private wheelchair-enabled transport with ramp access and experienced drivers trained to assist with boarding and safety. Q: Do you offer spiritual or wellness tours for disabled travelers? Yes, we can organize accessible spiritual tours in places like Varanasi, Rishikesh, or Kerala with adapted facilities. Q: Is the Taj Mahal accessible to wheelchairs? Yes. The Taj Mahal has wheelchair ramps and pathways, and wheelchairs can be made available upon request. Q: Can I bring my caregiver or assistant along? Yes, you’re welcome to travel with a caregiver, friend, or family member. We’ll make arrangements for both in hotel rooms and transport. Q: Do your packages include medical support or emergency care? We provide on-call doctor services and can arrange basic medical support if notified in advance. Q: Are monuments and heritage sites accessible in Rajasthan? Many palaces and forts in Rajasthan now offer partial access or view-only alternatives for wheelchair travelers. We adapt itineraries accordingly. Q: Do I need to bring my own wheelchair? If possible, yes. But we can also arrange wheelchair rental in India if informed in advance. Q: What activities are included for limited mobility guests? We focus on experiential tourism: cultural shows, cooking demos, heritage walks (optional), lakeside evenings, and light sightseeing. Q: Is airport assistance available for disabled travelers? Yes, we provide airport pick-up/drop with assistance, including help with luggage and wheelchair transfers. Q: Are the tours suitable for visually impaired or hearing-impaired travelers? Yes, with notice, we can provide sign language interpreters, audio guides, or tailored support. Q: How long are these accessible tours? Tours range from 5 to 12 days. The most popular is the 6-day Golden Triangle Accessible Tour and the 10-day Rajasthan Cultural Tour. Q: How can I book an Accessible India Tour? You can fill out our contact form or WhatsApp us directly. A travel advisor will get in touch to understand your needs and suggest a suitable itinerary. Credit : Sarthak Education trust Accessible Travel in India: Paving the Way for Inclusive Tourism Welcome to our guide on Accessible Travel in India – where we explore the advancements in making tourism more inclusive for travelers with disabilities. Over the years, India has made significant strides toward improving accessibility across its infrastructure, and we’re excited to showcase the key areas that are now equipped to host tourists with various challenges. ​ The Current State of Accessible Infrastructure in India ​ India’s tourism sector has made remarkable progress in addressing the needs of travelers with disabilities. While there is still room for improvement, many popular destinations and public spaces are now more accessible than ever. The country has focused on improving infrastructure, transport, accommodation, and tourism services to ensure a smoother travel experience for everyone. ​ Key Areas of Improvement: ​ Accessible Transport: Railways: Indian Railways has introduced dedicated coaches for differently-abled passengers, equipped with special seating arrangements, accessible toilets, and ramps. The \"Divyangjan\" initiative aims to make rail travel more comfortable for those with mobility challenges. Airports: Airports like Indira Gandhi International (Delhi), Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International (Mumbai), and Kempegowda International (Bengaluru) have implemented facilities such as ramps, accessible washrooms, dedicated assistance staff, and wheelchair services to support passengers with disabilities. Public Transport: Several major cities like Delhi, Bengaluru, and Mumbai have begun incorporating accessible buses and metro stations with elevators, ramps, and tactile paths for visually impaired travelers. Accommodation: Many hotels across India have adopted inclusive practices by providing wheelchair-friendly rooms, ramps, grab bars, and other amenities. Hotels in major cities like Delhi, Mumbai, and Goa, as well as at popular tourist destinations like Jaipur and Udaipur, are increasingly accommodating travelers with disabilities. Major hotel chains like Taj Hotels and Oberoi Hotels have pioneered in offering accessible rooms with facilities for those with physical challenges. Public Spaces & Tourist Attractions: Several popular tourist destinations have been modified to meet accessibility standards. For example, The Red Fort in Delhi and Qutub Minar have ramps, elevators, and wheelchairs available for visitors with mobility issues. Taj Mahal has also introduced ramps and wheelchair access, making it more accessible to all visitors. Beaches in Goa have beach wheelchairs, making it possible for travelers with mobility issues to experience the sands and surf. Medical Facilities and Emergency Assistance: Leading medical facilities across India are ensuring their buildings are accessible to people with disabilities. Hospitals in tourist destinations are now offering services and equipment, including wheelchairs, elevators, and assistance staff, to cater to emergency health needs. ​ Notable Organizations Pioneering Accessible Travel in India ​ The Wheelchair India Foundation: This organization focuses on providing mobility aids and raising awareness about accessible travel. They have been instrumental in advocating for accessible infrastructure and helping develop accessible tourism standards in India. Enable Travel (by TATA): Enable Travel is a specialized service by Tata that focuses on creating an inclusive travel experience for people with disabilities. They offer customized travel plans for people with mobility, visual, and other disabilities and have been working closely with the hospitality and transport sectors to ensure accessible services. Sarthak Educational Trust: Sarthak works toward empowering people with disabilities through education and employment. They also collaborate with the tourism industry to improve accessibility. Their efforts include accessible tourism advocacy and supporting individuals in traveling independently. Access India: A non-profit initiative working towards promoting accessible travel in India, Access India supports tourism development for people with disabilities. They work with various stakeholders to ensure that tourist destinations, hotels, and transportation options are accessible. ​ Prominent Accessible Tourist Destinations ​ Goa: Goa has increasingly become a popular destination for accessible tourism. The state has several beaches offering wheelchair access and hotels with accessible rooms. Special initiatives such as accessible beach shuttles, ramps, and toilets make Goa an inclusive destination. Jaipur: Jaipur, the capital of Rajasthan, has improved its accessibility in places like the Amber Fort, where wheelchair ramps and special viewing areas are available. The City Palace has also adopted accessible features for travelers with mobility challenges. Mumbai: Mumbai has several accessible public spaces like Marine Drive, Gateway of India, and Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj Vastu Sangrahalaya (Mumbai’s museum). These places have made provisions like ramps, tactile paths, and accessible washrooms. Kerala: Kerala’s backwaters, beaches, and hill stations have developed accessible facilities for travelers. Vypin Island in Kochi, for example, offers accessible boat rides, making the region more inclusive. ​ Future of Accessible Tourism in India ​ The future of accessible tourism in India looks promising. The government and private sector continue to push for further reforms, improvements, and collaborations to ensure that tourism becomes truly inclusive. With more stakeholders taking an active interest in accessibility, we can expect continued enhancements in infrastructure, services, and tourist experiences across the country. ​ Conclusion India is gradually becoming more accessible for tourists with disabilities, thanks to the collective efforts of governmental bodies, private organizations, and tourism service providers. While significant strides have been made, the journey is ongoing. Together, we can ensure that every traveler has the opportunity to explore the rich cultural heritage and diverse landscapes of India. For more information or to plan your next accessible travel experience, feel free to contact us. With 43 UNESCO World Heritage sites and the hospitality sector valued at $24 billion, the country is seeing an influx of domestic and international travellers. In sync with this, the government ...","content":"How An Accessible Travel, Tourism and Hospitality Sector Can Help Persons of Reduced Mobility in India Close Menu Facebook X (Twitter) Instagram Home Industry News New Appointments Culture Travel Tech Book Review Stories Tourism Wildlife Environment Interviews Hotel Review Green Warrior Follow the Food Jobs More E-Mag Contact Us Home » Travel Tech » How An Accessible Travel, Tourism and Hospitality Sector Can Help Persons of Reduced Mobility in India Travel Tech How An Accessible Travel, Tourism and Hospitality Sector Can Help Persons of Reduced Mobility in India By Jaun Shahi April 30, 2025 5 Mins Read Facebook Twitter LinkedIn WhatsApp Share Facebook Twitter LinkedIn WhatsApp Email By Sminu Jindal, Founder-Chairperson, Svayam India’s tourism industry is expanding rapidly, ranking 39th in the 2024 Travel and Tourism Development Index (TTDI) published by the World Economic Forum. With 43 UNESCO World Heritage sites and the hospitality sector valued at $24 billion, the country is seeing an influx of domestic and international travellers. In sync with this, the government has introduced various schemes to improve infrastructure, air connectivity, and sustainable tourism initiatives, however, a critical gap remains – accessibility for persons with reduced mobility. Despite ongoing developments, many individuals with mobility challenges still face obstacles while traveling. Uneven footpaths, inaccessible public transport, and a lack of proper facilities at tourist sites and hotels make travel difficult. Ensuring accessibility is not only about infrastructure, it is about creating a seamless and inclusive experience that allows everyone to explore the country without restrictions. Challenges in Accessibility For individuals with reduced mobility, planning a trip can often feel overwhelming due to unpredictable accessibility standards. Most public spaces, including airports, railway stations, and bus terminals, still lack essential features such as ramps, tactile pathways, and designated seating. While airlines provide wheelchair assistance, the journey beyond the airport often becomes difficult due to the absence of accessible taxis or last-mile connectivity. Tourist sites, including heritage monuments, pose another challenge. Many historical landmarks were built centuries ago, making modifications complex. While some efforts have been made to install ramps and provide alternate routes, several key sites remain difficult to access. Accommodation is another hurdle, with only a small fraction of hotels offering rooms that cater to wheelchair users. Even when such rooms exist, they are often limited in number and poorly designed. Beyond infrastructure, awareness among hospitality and tourism staff is a significant issue. Many professionals are not trained to assist PRMs effectively, leading to experiences that are uncomfortable and frustrating for travellers. This lack of sensitivity discourages many potential tourists from visiting India despite the country’s rich cultural and natural heritage. Addressing accessibility also makes economic sense. The global market for accessible tourism is growing, with aging populations and increased awareness driving demand for barrier-free travel. Countries that invest in inclusive tourism benefit from a broader customer base, attracting travellers who might otherwise hesitate to visit due to accessibility concerns. India’s tourism industry is projected to surpass $59 billion by 2028, and ensuring accessibility could significantly contribute to this growth. With the hospitality sector alone expected to reach $31 billion in the next five years, businesses that integrate accessibility measures will have a competitive edge. Additionally, making travel accessible creates job opportunities for individuals with disabilities within the sector, from customer service roles to tour guiding and hospitality management. Government Efforts and The Path Forward The Indian government has recognized the need for better tourism infrastructure and has introduced several initiatives to improve connectivity and sustainability. The RCS-UDAN Scheme, which has operationalized 579 tourism routes, is enhancing air travel access to remote destinations, making it easier for all travellers, including PRMs, to explore different parts of the country. The liberalization of e-visa facilities for 167 countries has also made international travel to India smoother, eliminating bureaucratic hurdles that could be challenging for those requiring special assistance. Another key initiative is Swadesh Darshan 2.0, which aims to develop sustainable tourism circuits with a focus on balancing environmental impact and development. The cruciality of making medical tourism for international travellers and religious tourism for Indian travellers more accessible should also not be overlooked due to these sectors’ contributions to the country’s economy. If accessibility is incorporated into this vision, it could transform India into a truly inclusive travel destination . However, for these efforts to have a tangible impact, accessibility must become a fundamental part of tourism policies rather than an afterthought. Making India an Inclusive Travel Destination To create a travel ecosystem that caters to all, accessibility must be incorporated at every stage of the tourism experience. Hotels and resorts should implement universal design principles, ensuring that accessible rooms, elevators, and public spaces become standard rather than an exception. Transport networks, including metros, buses, and taxis, need better last-mile solutions such as low-floor vehicles and designated pickup zones for PRMs. Heritage sites, while difficult to modify structurally, can benefit from thoughtful interventions such as audio guides, virtual reality experiences, and designated viewing areas. Museums and cultural centers should stress on creating inclusive experiences by providing sign language guides, tactile exhibits, and accessible restrooms. Awareness is equally important. Training programs for tourism and hospitality professionals can improve service quality for travellers with disabilities. By fostering an environment of inclusivity, India can position itself as a leader in accessible tourism, attracting a diverse range of visitors from around the world. As the country looks toward contributing $3 trillion to GDP from tourism by 2047, accessibility must be an integral part of this vision. A truly successful tourism industry is one that ensures that every traveller, regardless of mobility challenges, can experience the richness of India’s landscapes, heritage, and hospitality without barriers. The Philippines eTA for Indian citizens is a convenient online system that will allow travelers to easily obtain a permit to travel. They will be able to apply from the comfort of their homes or offices in a matter of minutes, without having to visit a Philippine embassy or consulate . Types of Philippines Visas for Indian Nationals Indian passport holders require a visa to enter the Philippines , regardless of the length of stay and the purpose of their trip. The Philippines offers a number of different visa types , including: A tourist visa to be used when traveling for tourism, leisure, or family visits A non-immigrant visa that can be used for working or studying in the country An immigrant visa for applicants with specific professional skills or qualifications that can benefit the country A resident retiree’s visa that allows permanent residence in the Philippines The Philippines diplomatic representations in India: embassy and consulates To obtain a visa for the Philippines, Indian nationals must visit the nearest Philippine embassy or consulate in person. The Philippine Embassy in India is located at the following address: 50-N Nyaya Marg Chanakyapuri New Delhi 110021 In addition to the embassy, the Philippines has three other representations in India: Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Chennai Spic House, Annex Building, VIII Floor 88 Mount Road Guindy Chennai 600032 Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Kolkata 37 Shakespeare Sarani 2nd Floor Kolkata Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Mumbai 159, 1st Floor, Industry House Churchgate Reclamation Mumbai 400020 Can Indian passport holders obtain a Philippines visa on arrival? The Philippines offers a visa on arrival to nationals of select countries. However, Indian travelers are not eligible for this type of visa . Indian travelers are required to obtain a visa suitable for the purpose of their trip before traveling to the Philippines . What Are the Philippines eTA Requirements for Indian Citizens? Indian passport holders must meet the Philippines eTA conditions for India in order to be able to apply for the travel authorization. All applicants must have an Indian passport with a minimum validity of 6 months after the date of leaving the Philippines. If the passport expires earlier, Indian candidates must renew the document and apply using the new passport. Likewise, dual nationals are required to enter the Philippines with the same passport they used to apply for an eTA. Additionally, it is necessary to have a valid debit or credit card to pay the eTA Philippines processing fee, as well as an email address to receive the eTA. It is important to keep in mind that eTA Philippines for Indian nationals is only valid for trips with the following purposes: tourism, leisure, and family or friends visits . How to Apply for a Philippines eTA from India To apply for a Philippines eTA from India, candidates will have to complete the Philippines’ online visa waiver application form and enter personal and passport details , including name, nationality, date of birth, passport number, and the expiry date of the passport. Applicants must make sure that all the information in the form is correct and corresponds to the details of the passport. It will also be necessary to pay a non-refundable eTA Philippines processing fee . Payment will be done through a secure online system using a credit or debit card authorized for online transactions. What should Indian candidates do after applying for a Philippines visa waiver? Indian applicants will need to print out the Philippines visa waiver and show it at the border crossing along with a valid passport. Travelers from India must also be able to present the following documents : A return or onward ticket Proof of sufficient funds to finance their trip Hotel booking Please note that the Philippines eTA for India will not automatically guarantee the right to enter the country . Philippine immigration officials may still refuse entry to passengers who are violating the Philippines’ immigration laws and regulations. Processing time for the Philippines eTA from India Indian travelers will obtain their eTA for the Philippines by email within three business days of applying . If there are any errors in the application, the process may take longer as applicants will be asked to fill out and submit a new form. The Philippine Embassies/Consulates will only accept electronic passports and machine readable passports for visa applications. Non-machine readable passports or hand-scripted passports will not be accepted for visa applications. The Visa Officer has the right to ask for any additional documents that may be deemed necessary. TYPES OF PHILIPPINE VISAS: Non-Immigrant Visas 9(a) Temporary Visitors Visa 9(a-1) - Business Visitors 9(a-2) - Tourism/Leisure Visitors 9(a-3) - Medical Treatment Visitors 9(a-4) - Private Yachtsmen/Sailors 9(a-5) - Filmmaking Visitors 9(a-6) - Journalism Visitors 9(a-7) - Athletic and Performing Arts Visitors 9(a-8) - Visitors with Other Purposes Free to enter the Philippines without Visa Visa-Required Nationals Non-Immigrant Visas 9(a) Temporary Visitors Visa for visitors coming for business, pleasure, medical treatment, training, attendance in conferences, meetings and workshops/seminars. Minimum Requirements: Passport/Travel Document Valid for at least six (6) months beyond the intended period of stay in the Philippines; Duly Accomplished Visa application form; Proof of bona fide status as tourist or businessman; Confirmed tickets for return or onward journey to the next port of destination; 9(a-1) - Business Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for scientific, commercial, industrial, professional and business purposes, who will not receive any compensation from a company/entity in the Philippines for services rendered in the country. Minimum Requirements for Business Owners: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Invitation letter from a Philippine government/private entity or endorsement from a Chamber of Commerce (recognized by the host government) in the country where the business is located; Proof of business operations/income; and Business permit/license or registration Bank statement of the latest six (6) months or tax payments Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging. Minimum Requirements for Employees: Certificate of Employment in a managerial level or higher; Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Endorsement letter from the applicant's company; Employment certificate with remuneration or letter of guarantee from the company/sponsor; and Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging. 9(a-2) - Tourism/Leisure Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for pleasure or touristic, leisure, religious, recreation, or amusement purposes, or to visit family, relatives, or friends, and staying for a predetermined amount of time. Minimum Requirements: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Proof of financial capacity (any of the following); Bank statement of the latest six (6) months or tax payments Employment certificate with remuneration Proof of business income Letter of guarantee from the sponsor with proof of financial capacity Documents proving ties to the home country/country of residence (any of the following); Employment certificate Property documents Itinerary; Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging; and For minors not travelling with a parent/guardian (additional requirement): Letter of permission from a parent/guardian 9(a-3) - Medical Treatment Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for medical treatment purposes or health reasons, including their escorts. Minimum Requirements: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Acceptance letter or confirmation of treatment or appointment from a Philippine medical or wellness institution; Proof of medical condition, such as medical abstract from the sending medical or wellness institution; Proof of financial means (any of the following) Bank statement of the latest six (6) months Pay slips for the latest six (6) months or employment certificate with remuneration (for employees) Pension statements of the latest six (6) months (for retirees) Receipt/confirmation of advance payment or deposit made for the treatment Letter of guarantee from the sponsor with proof of financial capacity Health insurance policy (if any) Hotel accommodation or proof lodging; For minors not travelling with a parent/guardian (additional requirement): Letter of permission from a parent/guardian For escort: Letter from the sending medical or wellness institution assigning of the foreign national to escort the patient 9(a-4) - Private Yachtsmen/Sailors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily on board a private yacht/sailboat; provided that such temporary visit will not entail an employer-employee relationship with a company/entity in the Philippines or the payment of any compensation in the Philippines. Minimum Requirements: Letter from the owner/captain indicating the following: Port or area of entry Date of entry and period of stay List of crew and passengers Particulars of the vessel: Type Name Registration Call sign Boat insurance policy; Cargo manifest; Character reference/shipping agent in the Philippines (if any); and For minors not travelling with a parent/guardian (additional requirement): Letter of permission from a parent/guardian 9(a-5) - Filmmaking Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for movie or documentary filming purposes; provided that such temporary visit will not entail an employer-employee relationship with a company/entity in the Philippines or the payment of any compensation in the Philippines. Minimum Requirements: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Letter of request addressed to the International Press Center; Background of film outfit; Curriculum Vitae of the filmmaker, including a shortlist of work credits; Synopsis/storyline of the film; Itinerary of activities; List of members of the crew with designations (if any); Employment certificate or contract from the film outfit; List of equipment with certificate from the film outfit that said equipment will be re-exported at the end of the trip to the Philippines; and Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging 9(a-6) - Journalism Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for news and journalism purposes; provided that such temporary visit will not entail an employer-employee relationship with a company/entity in the Philippines or the payment of any compensation in the Philippines. Minimum Requirements: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Letter of request addressed to the International Press Center; Background of media organization, or of contracting media organization (for freelance journalists); Curriculum Vitae of the newsman/journalist, including a shortlist of work credits; Synopsis/storyline/purpose of the news; Itinerary of activities; List of members of the crew with designations (if any); Employment certificate or contract from the media company; List of equipment with certificate from the media organization that said equipment will be re-exported at the end of the trip to the Philippines; and Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging 9(a-7) - Athletic and Performing Arts Visitors Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for athletic and performing arts purposes; provided that such temporary visit will not entail an employer-employee relationship with a company/entity in the Philippines or the payment of any compensation in the Philippines. Minimum Requirements: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Letter of request from the applicant's team, group, or organization; Invitation letter from Philippine Sports Commission, Philippine Olympic Committee, Games and Amusement Board, or a national sporting association duly recognized by the PSC/POC/GAB (for athletes); Endorsement letter from the Local Government Unit where the venue of the event is located (for performers); Contract or agreements with the organizer, including promotional materials; Itinerary of activities; Proof of financial means or letter of guarantee from the sponsor; List of members of the crew with designations (if applicable); and Employment certificate of crew members (if applicable); List of equipment with certification from the group that said equipment will be re-exported at the end of the trip to the Philippines (if applicable) Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging For minors not travelling with a parent/guardian (additional requirement): Letter of permission from a parent/guardian 9(a-8) - Visitors with Other Purposes Foreign nationals visiting the Philippines temporarily for purposes other than those cited above; provided that such temporary visit will not entail an employer-employee relationship with a company/entity in the Philippines or the payment of any compensation in the Philippines. Minimum Requirements for Visitors with Other Purposes: Tickets or flight reservation (entry and exit); Proof of financial capacity; Documents relative to the purpose of visit; Hotel accommodation or proof of lodging; and For minors not travelling with a parent/guardian (additional requirement): Letter of permission from a parent/guardian Free to enter the Philippines without Visa As governed by Executive Order No. 408 and the subsequent executive issuances amending it, the Department of Foreign Affairs maintains a list of eligible countries whose nationals may avail of visa-free entry to the Philippines provided that they are holders of passports valid for at least six (6) months beyond the contemplated period of stay. They must also possess return tickets to their country of origin or outward-bound tickets to their next country of destination. Nationals from the following countries are allowed to enter the Philippines without a visa for an initial stay of thirty (30) days: Andorra Angol Antigua and Barbuda Argentina Australia Austria Bahamas Bahrain Barbados Belgium Belize Benin Bhutan Bolivia Botswana Brazil* Brunei Darussalam Bulgaria Burkina Faso Burundi Cambodia Cameroon Canada Cape Verde Central African Republic Chad Chile Colombia Comoros Congo Costa Rica Cote d’Ivoire Croatia Cyprus Czech Republic Democratic Republic of the Congo Denmark Djibouti Dominica Dominican Republic Ecuador El Salvador Equatorial Guinea Eritre Estonia Ethiopia Fiji Finland France Gabon Gambia Germany Ghana Greece Grenada Guatemala Guinea Guinea Bissau Guyana Haiti Honduras Hungary Iceland Indonesia Ireland Israel* Italy Jamaica Japan Kazakhstan Kenya Kiribati Kuwait Kyrgyzstan Lao People’s Democratic Republic Latvia Lesotho Liberia Liechtenstein Lithuania Luxembourg Madagascar Malawi Malaysia Maldives Mali Malta Marshall Islands Mauritania Mauritius Mexico Micronesia Monaco Mongolia Morocco Mozambique Myanmar Namibia Nepal Netherlands New Zealand Nicaragua Niger Norway Oman Palau Panama Papua New Guinea Paraguay Peru Poland Portugal Qatar Republic of Korea Romania Russia Rwanda Saint Kitts and Nevis Saint Lucia Saint Vincent and the Grenadines Samoa San Marino Sao Tome and Principe Saudi Arabia Senegal Seychelles Singapore Slovak Republic Slovenia Solomon Islands South Africa Spain Suriname Swaziland Sweden Switzerland Tajikistan Thailand Togo Trinidad and Tobago Tunisia Turkey Turkmenistan Tuvalu Uganda United Arab Emirates United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland United Republic of Tanzania United States of America Uruguay Uzbekistan Vanuatu Vatican Venezuela Vietnam Zambia Zimbabwe Countries that are allowed to enter the Philippines visa-free for a stay of fifty-nine (59) days based on existing agreements. Brazil Israel Visa Policy for Indian Nationals In pursuit of its objective of enhancing tourism arrivals from India, the Philippine Government announces the following visa policies applicable to Indian nationals starting 08 June 2025: Indian nationals may enter the Philippines without a visa for a non-extendible and non-convertible period of 14 days for tourism purposes, upon presenting a passport valid for at least six (6) months beyond the contemplated stay, confirmed hotel accommodation / booking, proof of financial capacity, and return or onward ticket to the next country of destination. Indian nationals holding valid and current American, Japanese, Australian, Canadian, Schengen, Singapore or United Kingdom (AJACSSUK) visas or residence permits may enter the Philippines without a visa for a non-extendible period of 30 days for tourism, upon presenting a passport valid for at least six (6) months beyond the contemplated stay, and return or onward ticket to the next country of destination. These updated visa-free privileges for Indian nationals may be availed of at any Philippine port of entry, and are not convertible to a visa-based stay or other admission status categories. Indian nationals must also not have any derogatory record with the Bureau of Immigration (BI) in order to be admitted into the country without a visa. Indian nationals transiting in the Philippines or entering the country for long-term visits and non-tourism activities are required to apply for an appropriate Philippine visa at a Philippine Embassy or Consulate in their country or origin, place of legal residence, or any country that requires entry visas for Indian nationals. Visa-Required Nationals Nationals of other countries not indicated/included in the above-mentioned Executive order No. 408 are considered VISA-REQUIRED NATIONALS and will have to undergo more stringent set of documentary and other requirements per Philippine Codified Visa Rules and Regulations and all other relevant DFA Department Orders, Foreign Service Circulars and pertinent regulations. Visa Policy for Chinese Nationals Under FSC No. 112-11, the following may enter the Philippines without a visa for an initial stay not exceeding seven (7) days provided that they possess a return or onward ticket: 1) Holders of Hong Kong British passports; and 2) Mainland Chinese for tourism with a valid or unexpired Australian, Japanese, Canadian, Schengen or US visa. The 7-day visa-free entry may be extended by an additional fourteen (14) days to complete a maximum twenty-one (21) days. Hong Kong SAR passport holders may enter the Philippines visa-free for an initial stay of fourteen (14) days . Under FSC No. 122-11, Macau SAR passport holders may enter the Philippines without a visa for an initial stay of fourteen (14) days, provided, that they possess a return or onward ticket. Check requirements Philippines is now visa-free for Indians under these new categories; Check requirements The move, confirmed by the Embassy of the Philippines in New Delhi, is expected to boost tourist footfall from India, especially among those seeking scenic escapes across the archipelago's beaches and islands Join Our WhatsApp Channel Business Today Desk Updated May 27, 2025 5:31 PM IST Philippines offers 14- and 30-day visa-free entry options for eligible Indian travellers Indian tourists heading to the Philippines now have more flexibility than ever, with the country offering two new visa-free entry options in addition to its existing e-visa system. The move, confirmed by the Embassy of the Philippines in New Delhi, is expected to boost tourist footfall from India, especially among those seeking scenic escapes across the archipelago’s beaches and islands. Advertisement Visa-free options now available As of May 2025, Indian citizens can travel to the Philippines without a visa under two specific categories, based on their eligibility. 1. 14-day visa-free entry Indian passport holders can stay in the Philippines for up to 14 days for tourism without applying for a visa. However, this category is strictly non-extendable and cannot be converted into another visa type. Entry is permitted through all major airports, seaports, and even cruise terminals. Eligibility criteria: Travel must be for tourism purposes only Passport valid for at least six months beyond the stay Proof of accommodation and return or onward ticket Evidence of sufficient funds (e.g. bank statement, employment proof) Advertisement No negative immigration history in the Philippines 2. 30-day visa-free entry for AJACSSUK visa/residency holders Indian citizens holding valid visas or permanent residency from the following countries — Australia, Japan, America, Canada, Schengen states, Singapore, and the United Kingdom — are now eligible for a 30-day visa-free stay. Conditions include: Valid visa or PR from any AJACSSUK country Passport with six-month validity Confirmed return or onward travel Clean immigration record in the Philippines This, too, is a non-extendable, tourism-only provision. E-visa remains an option Indian travellers who don’t meet the visa-free eligibility can continue using the e-visa route. The 9(a) Temporary Visitor Visa, available through the official e-visa portal, allows a 30-day single-entry stay. Latest Facts & News About Philippines Visa on Arrival for Indians Planned tourist arrivals from India to the Philippines in 2025 require two critical updates about the Philippines visa for Indians system: The Philippines started offering e-visa applications officially in October 2024. This system exists to streamline visa applications for Indian citizens. The initiation of the visa system resulted in the approval of more than 7,000 Indian passport holders. Philippine authorities allow Indian citizens who hold valid visas from the United States, United Kingdom, Schengen Ar, Canada, or Australia to stay in the country visa-free for as long as 14 days. The Philippine visa-on-arrival system does not include Indians among its eligible nationalities. You must apply in advance. Useful Links Categories Canada Germany United Kingdom Miscellaneous Australia Frequently Asked Questions How long does it take to process a Philippines visa? The standard processing time for a regular tourist visa is about 8 to 10 working days. However, if you apply through the e-visa portal, the process is often faster and more convenient. Processing delays may happen during peak travel seasons or if your documents are incomplete. Can Indian citizens travel to the Philippines without a visa? Indian citizens can travel without a visa, but only under specific conditions. If you hold a valid visa for the US, UK, Schengen countries, Canada, or Australia, you are eligible for a 14-day visa-free stay in the Philippines. This benefit applies only if you’re entering directly from India or one of these qualifying countries. If you do not meet this condition, you must apply for a visa or e-visa before your trip. What is the validity period of a tourist visa? A single-entry tourist visa for the Philippines is valid for three months from the date it is issued. A multiple-entry tourist visa offers a more extended validity period of up to six months. It's important to note that this validity refers to how long you have to enter the country—not the duration you are allowed to stay inside the Philippines. Are there special requirements for business or student visas? Yes, there are. For a business visa, you must submit a letter of invitation from the host company in the Philippines, along with your own company's cover letter and proof of business registration. You’ll need proof of enrollment in a recognized Philippine institution for a student visa. You may also need academic transcripts, a No Objection Certificate (NOC) from your current college or school, and financial proof to support your education abroad. How can I extend my stay in the Philippines? If you want to extend your visit beyond the allowed duration, you must apply for an extension before your visa expires. To do this, visit the Bureau of Immigration office in the Philippines and submit the required application form, your passport, and the reason for the extension. Extensions are not guaranteed and are granted at the discretion of the immigration authorities. Additional fees will apply. To do so, you must also be wondering if it’s necessary to get a Philippines visa for Indians? Well, the answer is not quite simple. Under normal circumstances, any Indian entering the country must apply for a Philippines tourist visa for Indians. Anyone considering to go on vacation to the Philippines or cruising off the shore on the azure tranquility of the Pacific Ocean should know the details of Philippines visa process along with the required documentation and fees. Indian Embassy in the Philippines Frequently Asked Questions Do Indians Citizens Require a Visa to Enter Philippines? Yes, Indian citizens with regular passports need a visa to enter the Philippines for tourism and other purposes. Holders of diplomatic passports can enter the country without a visa for stays under 30 days. There are two visa-free entry options available for Indian nationals in the Philippines: Visa-Free Entry to Philippines for 14 Days Indian citizens can enter the Philippines without a visa for up to 14 days for tourism purposes if they meet the criteria which include a valid passport (at least 6 months beyond the intended stay), confirmed return or onward ticket, proof of accommodation (e.g., hotel booking), proof of financial capacity (e.g., bank statements) and no derogatory immigration record in the Philippines. Note: This entry is non-extendable and non-convertible to other visa types. It is only available through authorized international ports of entry. Visa-Free Entry to Philippines for 30 Days Indian nationals who possess a valid multiple-entry visa or permanent residence permit from countries such as the USA, Singapore, Schengen area nations, the UK, Australia, Canada, or Japan can enter the Philippines without a visa for up to 30 days. This stay can be extended by an additional 7 days. Is Visa-on-Arrival/E-Visa Available in the Philippines for Indians? Currently, the Philippines do not offer the option of visa-on-arrival for Indian nationals. As a result, you must apply for a visa before travelling to the country. With no availability of a Philippines visa on arrival for Indian citizens, it is advisable to apply for a visa as soon as you can. Generally, it takes 8-10 days to process an application. However, this might extend to a longer time period, depending on the embassy. Is Travel Insurance Mandatory for Philippines? No, travel insurance is not mandatory for your visit to the Philippines. However, it is highly recommended that you have travel insurance for the Philippines. Such a policy will cover any emergencies you might face, besides offering a financial net for illnesses, evacuations, or lost baggage. Different Types of Philippines Visa for Indians and Their Validity Check out the most common types of visa for Philippines from India, along with their number of entries, duration of stay, and validity period. Visa Type Number of Entries Stay Duration (Per Entry) Validity Period Purpose Tourist Visa Single or Multiple Up to 30 days (can be extended) Single Entry: 30 days Multiple Entry: 6 months or 1 year Short-term tourism, social visits, attending conferences/workshops/training sessions. Business Visa Single or Multiple Up to 60 days (may vary depending on approval) Single Entry: 6 months Multiple Entry: 1 Year Business meetings, negotiations, conferences (does not permit local employment). Transit Visa Single Up to 72 hours Not applicable Passing through the Philippines to another destination. Student Visa Single or Multiple Varies depending on the program duration 1 year, can be extended Full-time studies in an accredited Philippine educational institution. Pre-arranged Employment Visa Single Up to 30 days 1, 2 or 3 years To take up employment with a pre-approved Philippine company. Diplomatic Visa Multiple Varies depending on the agreement between countries Varies depending on the agreement between countries Issued to diplomats, government officials, and their dependents. Note: The extension of visas is subject to approval by Philippines immigration authorities and additional fees may apply. The visa validity period is different from the period of stay. Philippines Visa Fees for Indian Citizens There are quite a few types of Philippines visa for Indian passport holders depending on the duration for which it is being applied. While it is unlikely that an individual would avail any of the long-time options as a tourist; the options and their fees are mentioned in the table below: Type of Visa Fee in INR Single entry for 3 months ₹3520 Multiple entry for 6 Months ₹7040 Multiple entry for 1 year ₹10,560 Transit Visa ₹2540 Disclaimer: The visa fees mentioned above cover a processing period of 10 days. For express processing within 6 days, an additional fee of ₹880 is required. This fee should be paid via a demand draft made out to the Embassy of the Philippines and is due upon passport collection. The Philippines visa fees for Indian passport holders as well as its structure are regularly subject to change as per revisions of Philippines embassy guidelines. Subsequently, tourists should check the embassy website for relevant fees related details before applying for it. Eligibility Criteria for Philippines Tourist Visa for Indians To avail a temporary visitor’s visa, you can apply at the Philippine Embassy or Consulate in your own country of origin or legal residence. The minimum requirements for applying for the Philippines tourist visa include: A valid passport or travel document with at least 6 months validity beyond the intended period of stay in the Philippines Duly filled visa application forms Passport sized photos Proof of identity as a tourist or businessman Confirmed round-trip tickets or tickets of onward journey to a third country Proof of payment of visa fees Documents Required by Indians for Philippines Tourist Visa Application The different documents which are necessary to fulfil the Philippines tourist visa requirements for Indian citizens are listed below. Valid Passport: Indian passport which must be valid for at least 6 months. Your past passports and visas may also be required. Application Form: Visa application form which must be duly filled in English. Photograph: 2 passport-sized coloured photographs taken not more than 6 months before. Cover Letter: Letter from the applicant describing the purpose of his/her visit. Financial Proof: Provide a copy of your credit cards and/or traveler’s checks, or include a foreign exchange endorsement on your passport. Bank statement of 6 last months and your salary slips of last 3 months prior to application are also required. Invitation Letter: Submit the original, signed letter from the inviting party, including their complete address and telephone number. The letter must be notarized in the Philippines. Tickets: Your hotel booking and flight ticket details will be required. Employment Verification: For employed applicants, provide a Certificate of Current Employment from your employer, detailing the employer’s full name, address, and telephone number, along with your length of service and salary details. Self-Employment Verification: If self-employed, submit a photocopy of your latest Income Tax Return and a copy of your Personal Assessment Number (PAN) Card. Student Verification: For students, provide a Certificate of Enrollment or a Letter of Acceptance from your school. Spouse of a Philippine Citizen: I f you are the spouse of a Philippine citizen, ensure that your Filipino spouse’s name is indicated in your passport. Provide a copy of the Report of Marriage issued by the Philippine Embassy or the Philippine National Statistics Office. Unmarried Minor Child of a Filipino Parent: For unmarried minor children of a Filipino parent, provide a copy of the birth certificate and a copy of the parent’s valid passports. Minor Travel Authorization: For minors (under 18 years of age) traveling alone or with someone other than a parent, provide an Affidavit of Support and Consent to Travel. This affidavit must be signed by either or both parents, indicating the minor's traveling companion and the address in the Philippines. It must be notarized by a local Notary Public, attested by the MEA, and consular officer of the Philippine Embassy. Present this affidavit to immigration officials upon arrival in the Philippines. Among Philippines visa requirements for minors with Indian passport, further documents regarding leave and other necessities from school or college are necessary as well. Note: Please be notified that additional documents may be asked for at the consular office to support your application apart from those mentioned in the above list of documents required for Philippines visa. How to Apply for Philippines Visa from India? You will need to apply for Philippines visa from India offline. After arranging the document and Philippines tourist visa fees for Indian citizens, you can easily apply for it yourself or employ a broker or travel agent.  \r\n\r\nIf you are applying for it yourself, you must visit the Philippines embassy or consulates in New Delhi, Kolkata, Mumbai, or Chennai. The steps include: Download & Fill Out Philippines Visa Application Form You will need to download the application form from the official website of Philippines embassy, print it out and fill in the form. Submit Your Philippines Visa Form for Indians at Embassy Once you fill up the form accurately, submit it with all documents to the embassy or your nearest consular office. No prior appointment is required. However, you can contact them in advance to confirm their availability. Make Payment of Your Philippines Tourist Visa Fee Settle your visa fee through demand drafts drawn from a bank. Remember that cash payment is not accepted. How to Apply for Philippines Visa Through an Agent? In case you do not want to go through the hassle of applying for your Philippines visa for Indian citizens yourself, you can avail the services of a travel agent or a broker. Generally, they collect the documents and your personal information to apply for the visa. Availing the service of a broker or a travel agent will also require additional charges. With the option for a Philippines visa on arrival for Indian passport holders not being available as of yet, you should apply for the visa approval well ahead of the vacation. What is the Processing Time for Philippines Visa for Indians? The processing time for Philippines visa applications is around 10 working days. Unless there are unexpected issues or extra documents needed, you can pick up your passport on the eighth working day after you submit it. Processing doesn't include Saturdays, Sundays, and holidays in the Philippines or India. Things You Must Know Before Applying for Philippines Visa from India You need to submit a temporary visitor's visa application in person. A responsible adult can submit the application for minors under 18, as approved by the consular officer. Minors, except for infants in arms, should attend the Consulate interview with the adult applying on their behalf. You should complete the electronic Customs Baggage Declaration Form (e-CBDF) and the electronic Currencies Declaration Form (e-CDF) using the e-Travel website or the eGovPH app, within 72 hours before arriving or leaving. If you bring in or take out more than USD 10,000, or its equivalent or you carry more than PhP 50,000 into or out of the Philippines, you must mention these in the e-CDF. If a visa application is denied, or if it is approved after the applicant’s intended departure date, there will be no visa fee refund. List of Philippines Embassy and Consulate in India Embassy/Consulate City Official Site Embassy of the Philippines New Delhi https://newdelhipe.dfa.gov.ph/ Consulate General of the Philippines Mumbai NA Consulate General of the Philippines Kolkata NA Consulate General of the Philippines Chennai NA List of Indian Embassy in Philippines Contact City Official Site Embassy of India in Philippines Manila https://www.eoimanila.gov.in/ What Could Cause the Denial of a Philippines Tourist Visa Application? In case any Indian has a maligned past record with the Philippine Bureau of Immigration, he/she might be denied entry. Additionally, if key aspects such as strong ties to your home country, enough financial resources for your stay in the Philippines, and a clear intention to follow the rules of a temporary visitor's visa are not adequately demonstrated, the visa application can be denied. Your application can also be rejected due to suspicion of misrepresentation, submission of fraudulent documents, inconsistencies between your application form and supporting documents, and failure to submit pending documents within the given 5-day period. What Happens if My Philippines Visa Application is Rejected? If your visa application for the Philippines from India is rejected, you have the option to reapply. It is recommended that you fully understand the reasons for rejection as communicated by the consular office, address these issues, and then reapply for the visa. You should wait at least 6 months before reapplying. How Can I Avoid Getting My Philippines Visa Application Rejected? You can lessen the likelihood of your Philippines visa application being rejected by ensuring you adhere to the following guidelines: Meet Eligibility Criteria: Make sure you meet both the general and specific requirements for the type of visa you are applying for. Complete Application Carefully: Verify that your application form is filled out accurately and, in its entirety, leaving no sections incomplete. Valid and Genuine Documents: Submit all required documents as specified by the embassy, ensuring they are valid, genuine, and accompanied by clear copies. Proof of Adequate Funds: Show evidence that you have sufficient financial means to cover your expenses during your stay in the Philippines. Onward/Return Ticket: Provide a confirmed ticket for your return or onward journey, highlighting your planned exit from the Philippines. Accommodation Details: Offer confirmation of your accommodation arrangements for your visit. Can I Extend My Philippines Visa While I'm in Philippines? No, you cannot extend your stay in the Philippines if you travel visa-free for up to 14 days trip. If you travel visa-free with a valid multiple-entry visa or permanent residence permit from other countries such as USA, Singapore, the Schengen area, the United Kingdom, Australia, Canada, or Japan for up to 30 days, then your stay can be extended for up to 7 days. To extend your Philippines visa, you need to visit the Philippine Bureau of Immigration, Manila, or their field/regional office. Should You Buy Travel Insurance for Philippines? International Travel insurance is recommended for your trip to the Philippines. Availing travel insurance while beginning the visa process for the Philippines from India can ensure it is effectively ready during your trip with the following basic benefits. Adventure sports are a must-to-do activity in Philippines and despite being safely controlled, they can suddenly turn rogue. Consequently, any emergency due to a single day adventure sports is also covered by insurance providers. You might be in an accident during a road trip where you end up injuring another individual or damaging his/her property. FAQs Do Indians Need a Philippines Visa? Yes, Indian citizens are eligible for visa-free entry to the Philippines for up to 14 days. Indian citizens who wish to stay in the Philippines for more than 2 weeks can apply for an eVisa to enter the country. Visa Name Entry Type Stay Duration Validity Fees (PHP/INR) Processing Time Form 14-Day Visa-Free Entry Single 14 days 14 days – On Arrival Stamp 30-Day Visa-Free Entry Single Up to 30 days Up to 30 days – On Arrival Stamp 9(a) Temporary Visitors Visa Single Entry Up to 59 days 3 months PHP 3,608 / INR 5,560 7-12 working days eVisa 9(a) Temporary Visitors Visa Multiple Entry Up to 59 days 6 Months PHP 5829/ INR 8,983 15-21 working days eVisa 9(a) Temporary Visitors Visa Multiple Entry Up to 59 days 1 year PHP 8049/ INR 12,403 15-21 working days eVisa Philippines Visa Eligibility Criteria for Indians To be eligible for a Philippines tourist visa, Indian applicants must meet the following requirements: Valid Passport : Hold a valid Indian passport with at least 6 months’ validity from the intended date of arrival in the Philippines. Ensure your passport has at least two blank pages for visa stamps. Proof of Financial Capability : Provide bank statements or other financial documents proving you have sufficient funds to cover your expenses during your stay in the Philippines. Confirmed Travel Arrangements : Submit confirmed round-trip flight tickets and hotel reservations or a detailed itinerary of your planned stay. Purpose of Visit : Clearly state your purpose of visit as tourism or leisure. If invited by a Filipino citizen or organization, provide an invitation letter with their contact details. No Criminal Record : Has no history of criminal offenses or immigration violations. Philippines Visa Fees for Indians Here’s a breakdown of the Philippines tourist visa fees for Indians: Type of Visa Visa Duration Fees in PHP Fees in INR 9(a) Tourist Visa – Single Entry Up to 59 days PHP 3,608 INR 5,560 9(a) Tourist Visa – Multiple Entry Up to 59 days PHP 5,829 INR 8,983 9(a) Tourist Visa – Multiple Entry Up to 59 days PHP 8,049 INR 12,403 Philippines Visa Requirements & Documents for Indians When applying for a Philippines tourist visa, Indian citizens must submit the following documents: Visa Application Form Duly completed online visa application . Attach a recent passport-size photograph (45mm x 35mm) taken within the last three months. Personal Documents Valid Indian passport with at least 6 months’ validity from the intended date of arrival in the Philippines. A photocopy of the passport’s bio-page and all pages with visa stamps. Proof of financial capacity, such as: Original bank statements for the last six months. Income tax returns for the previous year. Pay slips or proof of employment. Confirmed round-trip flight tickets. Hotel reservations or a detailed itinerary of your planned stay in the Philippines. Must Read: Philippines Visa Photo Requirements for Indians Financial Documents Bank statements for the last 6 months show sufficient funds. Income Tax Returns (ITR) for the previous year. Credit card statements, traveler’s checks, or forex cards. Employment and Business Documents Leave approval letter from the employer. Employment contract and salary slips for the last 3 months. Business registration certificate and proof of business transactions for self-employed applicants. Additional Documents for Minors Birth certificate of the minor applicant. Passport copies and visa copies of both parents. No objection affidavit from non-traveling parents, attested by a notary. Supporting Documents (if applicable) If employed: Certificate of employment stating your position, length of service, and approved leave of absence. If self-employed: Business registration documents and income tax returns. If a student: School certificate and approved leave of absence. If invited by a Filipino citizen or organization: Invitation letter with the inviter’s contact details and a photocopy of their government-issued ID. Philippines Visa Process for Indians Follow these step-by-step instructions to apply for a Philippines tourist visa from India: Step 1: Access the official website for the visa application. Step 2: Register using a valid email address. Set a password to create your account Step 3 : Review the details for your type of visa and scroll down to the bottom of the page to start the application. Select ‘New Application’. Step 4: Complete the details about the type of visa, select the Embassy or Consulate for your region in India, and provide your email address. Provide personal details, including name, date of birth, and nationality. Enter passport information (passport number, issuance/expiry dates). Specify travel plans, including intended dates of arrival and departure. Indicate the purpose of your visit (e.g., tourism) Step 5: Upload required documents: Prepare digital copies of the following: A passport-sized photograph. The biodata page of your passport. Proof of accommodation (hotel booking or invitation letter). A return or onward travel ticket. Evidence of sufficient funds for your stay (e.g., bank statement) Step 6: Pay the visa fee. Submit payment securely online via direct bank transfer or other available methods. Ensure payment is completed within 48 hours of application submission to avoid delays Step 7: After submission, you will receive an acknowledgment receipt with a unique reference number. Use this number to track your Philippines visa application status online. Processing times may vary, so apply well in advance of your travel date. Step 8: Once your visa is processed, you will be notified via email or SMS. Print a copy of the approved eVisa or save it on your mobile device. Present it at immigration upon arrival in the Philippines. Must Read: How to check the Philippines’ visa status online Philippines Visa Processing Time for Indians The typical processing time for a Philippines tourist visa for Indians: Type of Visa Processing Time 9(a) Tourist Visa (single entry) 7-12 working days 9(a) Tourist Visa (multiple entries) 15-21 working days Philippines Stay Duration, Validity, and Extension for Indians A Philippine tourist visa allows Indian citizens to stay in the country for up to 59 days per visit. The visa is valid for 3 months from the date of issue, which means you must enter the Philippines within this period. Visa Stay Duration Validity Extension 9(a) Tourist Visa – Single Entry Up to 59 days 3 months from the issue date Non Extendable The Philippine eVisa is non-extendable, meaning you cannot apply for a stay extension once you have entered the country using an eVisa. You must leave the Philippines before your visa expires, as overstaying may result in fines or future entry restrictions. Must Read: Philippines Visa Extension for Indians Top Mistakes While Applying for a Philippines Visa To ensure a smooth visa application process, avoid these common mistakes: Incomplete or Incorrect Application Form : Missing or inaccurate details can lead to delays or rejection. Double-check all information before submission. Insufficient Passport Validity : Your passport must be valid for at least six months beyond your intended stay, with enough blank pages for visa stamping. Lack of Financial Proof : Applicants must demonstrate financial capability by providing bank statements, salary slips, or sponsorship letters. Insufficient funds can result in rejection. Missing or Incorrect Travel Itinerary : A well-planned itinerary, including flight reservations and hotel bookings, is required. Inconsistent or vague travel plans may cause application issues. Failure to Provide an Invitation Letter (if required) : If visiting family or friends, an invitation letter from the host, along with their ID proof and financial documents, may be needed. Climate and Top Season The Philippines enjoys a tropical climate, with warm temperatures throughout the year. The country experiences two main seasons: the dry season from November to April and the wet season from May to October. The best time to visit the Philippines is during the dry season, particularly from December to February, when the weather is pleasant and rainfall is minimal. Remember to pack light, breathable clothing, sunscreen, and a light rain jacket or umbrella, depending on the season. Culture The Philippines boasts a rich cultural heritage that reflects its diverse history and influences from Spanish, American, and Asian cultures. The country is predominantly Catholic, and religious festivals and traditions play a significant role in Filipino life. One of the most famous celebrations is the Sinulog Festival, held every January in Cebu City, which honors the Santo Niño (Holy Child Jesus) with colorful street dancing and parades. Other notable festivals include the Ati-Atihan Festival in Aklan, the Dinagyang Festival in Iloilo, and the Moriones Festival in Marinduque. These events showcase the vibrant costumes, music, and dances that are integral to Filipino culture. Attractions The Philippines offers a wide array of attractions that cater to every type of traveler. Here are some must-visit destinations: Boracay Island is known for its stunning white sand beaches, crystal-clear waters, and vibrant nightlife. Palawan: Home to the Puerto Princesa Underground River, a UNESCO World Heritage Site, and the breathtaking islands of El Nido and Coron. Bohol: Famous for the Chocolate Hills, a unique geological formation, and the adorable tarsiers, one of the world’s smallest primates. Siargao Island: A surfer’s paradise, offering world-class waves and laid-back island vibes. Vigan: A UNESCO World Heritage City, renowned for its well-preserved Spanish colonial architecture and cobblestone streets. Manila: The bustling capital city, featuring historical landmarks like Intramuros and modern attractions like the SM Mall of Asia. Activities The Philippines offers a plethora of activities for adventure seekers and nature lovers alike. Some must-try experiences include: Island hopping: Explore the country’s stunning islands and discover hidden beaches, lagoons, and snorkeling spots. Scuba diving: The Philippines is a top diving destination, with world-renowned sites like the Tubbataha Reefs Natural Park and the Apo Reef Natural Park. Surfing: Catch some waves in Siargao, known as the surfing capital of the Philippines, or in other popular spots like La Union and Baler. Hiking: Trek through lush rainforests, climb volcanic mountains like Mount Pinatubo, or explore the Banaue Rice Terraces, a UNESCO World Heritage Site. Food tripping: Indulge in delicious Filipino cuisine, from the famous adobo and sinigang to street food favorites like balut and kwek-kwek. Must Read: Discover the Enchanting Nightlife in the Philippines: Your Passport to Unforgettable Adventures! Cost To Visit the Philippines On average, a budget-friendly trip can cost around INR 50,000 to INR 70,000 per person for a week, including airfare, accommodation, food, and some activities. Mid-range travelers can expect to spend around INR 80,000 to INR 1,20,000 per person, while luxury travelers may spend upwards of INR 1,50,000 per person. Why Choose OneVasco? If you plan to stay in the Philippines for longer, you can opt for an eVisa through the official portal. 3. Is a Philippine visa easy to get for Indians? Yes, the Philippines is visa-free for Indians, and an eVisa facility is also available. The latter process is digital and easy to understand and complete. 4. What are the Philippines visa fees for Indian visitors? The Philippines tourist visa fees for Indians are approximately INR 5,560 for a single entry (30 days) and INR 8983 for a 6-month visa. 5. Are children eligible to apply for a Philippines visa? Yes, children of all ages require a valid Philippines visa for Indians to enter the country. They must apply separately, even if they are included in their parents’ passports. 9. What’s the process for obtaining a Philippines visa for Indians? The Philippines visa application process for India involves: Filling out the visa application form. Gathering the required Philippines visa documents for Indians.
