As an Indian citizen planning a tourist trip to the Philippines, you have a few options depending on your existing visas and planned duration of stay. Here's a comprehensive guide to help you:\n\n### **Visa Requirements for Indian Citizens Traveling to the Philippines for Tourism**\n\n**1. Visa-Free Entry Options (Effective May 2025):**\n\nThe Philippines offers two visa-free entry options for Indian citizens under specific conditions:\n\n*   **14-Day Visa-Free Entry:**\n    *   **Eligibility:**\n        *   Your travel must be strictly for tourism purposes.\n        *   You must hold an Indian passport valid for at least six months beyond your intended stay in the Philippines.\n        *   You need to show proof of confirmed accommodation (e.g., hotel booking) and a return or onward ticket to your next destination.\n        *   You must provide evidence of sufficient funds to cover your expenses during your trip (e.g., bank statements, employment proof).\n        *   You should not have any derogatory immigration record in the Philippines.\n    *   **Important Note:** This 14-day visa-free stay is **non-extendable** and **cannot be converted** into another visa type. Entry is permitted through all major airports, seaports, and cruise terminals.\n\n*   **30-Day Visa-Free Entry for AJACSSUK Visa/Residency Holders:**\n    *   **Eligibility:**\n        *   You must hold a valid and current multiple-entry visa or permanent residence permit from one of the following countries: **A**ustralia, **J**apan, **A**merica (USA), **C**anada, **S**chengen states, **S**ingapore, or the **U**nited **K**ingdom (AJACSSUK).\n        *   Your Indian passport must be valid for at least six months beyond your intended stay.\n        *   You need a confirmed return or onward ticket.\n        *   You must have a clean immigration record in the Philippines.\n    *   **Important Note:** This 30-day visa-free stay is also primarily for tourism and is generally non-extendable, though some sources mention a possible 7-day extension at the discretion of immigration authorities.\n\n**2. E-Visa (9(a) Temporary Visitor Visa):**\n\nIf you do not meet the criteria for visa-free entry or wish to stay longer than the permitted visa-free periods, you will need to apply for a visa. The Philippines launched an official e-visa system in October 2024, which streamlines the application process.\n\n*   **Purpose:** Ideal for leisure trips, vacations, or visiting friends and family.\n*   **Validity & Stay Duration:**\n    *   Single-entry: Valid for 3 months, allowing a stay of up to 59 days.\n    *   Multiple-entry: Valid for 6 months or 1 year, allowing a stay of up to 59 days per entry.\n*   **Processing Time:**\n    *   E-Visa (Tourist): 5 to 8 working days.\n    *   Traditional Tourist Visa (sticker visa): 8 to 10 working days for single entry, 15-21 working days for multiple entries.\n*   **Fees (Approximate, subject to change):**\n    *   Single-entry Tourist (3 months validity): INR 3,520 (approx. PHP 3,608)\n    *   Multiple-entry Tourist (6 months validity): INR 7,040 (approx. PHP 5,829)\n    *   Multiple-entry Tourist (1 year validity): INR 10,560 (approx. PHP 8,049)\n    *   Express Processing (optional): An additional INR 1,000–2,000.\n    *   **Note:** Visa fees are non-refundable, even if your application is rejected.\n\n### **How to Apply for a Philippines E-Visa:**\n\nThe e-visa system allows you to apply online without physically visiting an embassy or consulate.\n\n1.  **Access the Official Website:** Go to the official e-Visa application portal: `https://www.visa.gov.ph`\n2.  **Create an Account:** Register using a valid email address and set up a secure password.\n3.  **Complete the Application Form:** Log in and select \"New Application.\" Fill out the online form with your personal details, passport information, and travel itinerary. Ensure all information is accurate and matches your passport.\n4.  **Upload Required Documents (Digital Copies):**\n    *   A clear passport-sized photograph (45mm x 35mm, white background, neutral expression, no eyeglasses, taken within the last six months).\n    *   A clear copy of your passport's bio-page (the page with your personal details).\n    *   Proof of accommodation (hotel booking confirmation or an invitation letter from a host in the Philippines).\n    *   Confirmed round-trip flight tickets or onward journey tickets.\n    *   Evidence of sufficient funds to cover your stay (e.g., bank statements for the last 3-6 months, income tax returns, salary slips, credit card copies, foreign exchange endorsement, or a letter of guarantee from a sponsor with their financial proof).\n    *   A cover letter describing your travel itinerary, purpose of visit, duration of stay, occupation, and how you will cover your expenses.\n    *   **For employed individuals:** Certificate of Current Employment from your employer, detailing your position, length of service, and salary.\n    *   **For self-employed individuals:** Company registration documents, latest Income Tax Return (ITR), and a copy of your Personal Assessment Number (PAN) Card.\n    *   **For students:** Student ID, Certificate of Enrollment, or Letter of Acceptance from your school/college.\n    *   **For minors (under 18) traveling alone or with someone other than a parent:** Birth certificate, passport copies of both parents, a notarized No Objection Affidavit from non-traveling parents, and an Affidavit of Support and Consent to Travel.\n5.  **Pay the Visa Fee:** Make the payment securely online using a credit or debit card. Ensure payment is completed within 48 hours of application submission.\n6.  **Submit Application:** Review all details and submit your application.\n7.  **Track Your Application:** You will receive an acknowledgment receipt with a unique reference number. Use this number to track your application status on the e-Visa portal.\n8.  **Receive E-Visa:** Once approved, your e-visa will be sent to your registered email address as a PDF document. Print a copy and save it on your mobile device to present at immigration upon arrival.\n\n### **Important Considerations:**\n\n*   **eTravel Document:** All Indian passengers must obtain the eTravel document (Health Declaration Form) before entering the Philippines. You can apply for this online.\n*   **Visa on Arrival:** Indian citizens are generally **not eligible** for a visa on arrival in the Philippines, with the exception of those holding valid AJACSSUK visas/residency permits for a 30-day stay.\n*   **Travel Insurance:** While not mandatory, travel insurance is highly recommended to cover unforeseen medical emergencies, trip cancellations, or lost baggage.\n*   **Immigration Discretion:** An approved visa or e-visa does not automatically guarantee entry. Philippine immigration officials at the port of entry have the final say on admission.\n*   **Overstaying:** Overstaying your permitted duration can lead to fines and future entry restrictions.\n\n### **Philippine Embassies/Consulates in India (for traditional sticker visa applications, if needed):**\n\nIf you prefer to apply for a traditional sticker visa or if your travel purpose requires it, you can visit one of the following Philippine diplomatic missions in India:\n\n*   **Embassy of the Philippines in New Delhi:**\n    *   Address: 50-N Nyaya Marg, Chanakyapuri, New Delhi 110021\n    *   Phone: +91-11-2688-9091\n    *   Website: [http://newdelhipe.dfa.gov.ph/](http://newdelhipe.dfa.gov.ph/)\n*   **Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Chennai:**\n    *   Address: Spic House, Annex Building, VIII Floor, 88 Mount Road, Guindy, Chennai 600032\n    *   Phone: +91-44-2235 4063\n*   **Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Kolkata:**\n    *   Address: 37 Shakespeare Sarani, 2nd Floor, Kolkata\n    *   Phone: +91-33-2280 8846 / +91-33-2280 2546\n*   **Philippine Honorary Consulate General in Mumbai:**\n    *   Address: 159, 1st Floor, Industry House, Churchgate Reclamation, Mumbai 400020\n\n**Next Steps:**\n\n1.  **Determine your eligibility:** Check if you qualify for the 14-day or 30-day visa-free entry based on your passport validity, existing visas, and travel purpose.\n2.  **Gather Documents:** Prepare all the necessary documents as listed above.\n3.  **Apply:**\n    *   If eligible for visa-free entry, ensure you have all supporting documents (return ticket, accommodation, funds) ready for immigration.\n    *   If you need a visa, apply through the official e-Visa portal (`https://www.visa.gov.ph`) for a faster process.\n4.  **Complete eTravel Document:** Fill out the mandatory eTravel document before your departure.\n\nBy following these guidelines, you should have a smooth process for your tourism trip to the That will cover your entry into the Philippines. However, since you have a layover in Singapore, you need to consider Singapore's transit visa requirements as an Indian citizen.\n\nHere's what you need to know:\n\n**Singapore Transit Visa for Indian Citizens**\n\nGenerally, Indian citizens do **not** need a transit visa for Singapore if they meet certain conditions, primarily related to the **Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF)**.\n\n**1. Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF) - Up to 96 Hours:**\n\nYou may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you meet these requirements:\n\n*   **Transit to/from a third country:** You must be in transit to or from a third country (meaning, Singapore is not your final destination, and you are not returning directly to India from Singapore after using the VFTF).\n*   **Onward/Return Ticket:** You must have a confirmed onward air or sea ticket departing Singapore within 96 hours.\n*   **Valid Visa/Long-Term Pass from Specific Countries:** You must hold a valid visa or long-term pass (with at least one month's validity from the date of entry into Singapore under the VFTF) issued by one of the following countries:\n    *   Australia\n    *   Canada\n    *   Germany\n    *   Japan\n    *   New Zealand\n    *   Switzerland\n    *   United Kingdom\n    *   United States of America\n*   **Passport Validity:** Your Indian passport must be valid for at least six months beyond your date of arrival in Singapore.\n*   **Sufficient Funds:** You should have sufficient funds to cover your expenses during your stay in Singapore.\n*   **Clean Immigration Record:** You should not have any previous immigration offenses in Singapore or other countries.\n\n**Important Notes on VFTF:**\n\n*   **Entry is at the discretion of immigration officers:** Even if you meet all the criteria, the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at the point of entry have the final say on your admission.\n*   **One-time use:** The VFTF is generally valid for only one leg of your journey (either onward or return, but not both).\n*   **No extension:** Extension of stay under VFTF is strictly not allowed.\n*   **Airside Transit:** If your layover is short and you remain within the international transit area of Changi Airport without clearing immigration, you generally do not need any visa, regardless of VFTF eligibility. This is common if your connecting flight is on the same reservation or airline system and your baggage is checked through to your final destination (Philippines).\n\n**2. When You Might Need a Standard Singapore Visa:**\n\nYou would need to apply for a standard Singapore visa if:\n\n*   You do not meet the VFTF requirements (e.g., you don't have a valid visa from one of the listed countries, or your layover exceeds 96 hours).\n*   You intend to leave the airport for sightseeing or need to transit between terminals that require you to clear immigration, and you are not eligible for VFTF.\n*   Your flights are on separate tickets, and you need to collect and re-check your baggage, which would require you to clear immigration.\n\n**In your specific situation:**\n\nYou mentioned you have a flight from Singapore to the Philippines with Star Airline.\n\n*   **Check your layover duration:** How long is your layover in Singapore?\n*   **Check your baggage situation:** Is your baggage checked through directly from India to the Philippines, or do you need to collect it in Singapore and re-check it for your Star Airline flight?\n*   **Check if you intend to leave the airport:** Do you plan to exit the airport and explore Singapore during your layover?\n\n**Recommendations:**\n\n1.  **Confirm Baggage Policy with Air India and Star Airline:**\n    *   Contact Air India to confirm if your baggage will be checked through all the way to the Philippines. If it is, and your layover is short, you might not need to clear immigration in Singapore.\n    *   If your baggage is not checked through, you will likely need to clear immigration in Singapore to collect and re-check it.\n2.  **Assess VFTF Eligibility:**\n    *   Since you have a Philippines visa, that doesn't count as a visa from one of the \"AJACSSUK\" countries for Singapore's VFTF.\n    *   Therefore, unless you also hold a valid visa or long-term pass from Australia, Japan, America (USA), Canada, Schengen states, Singapore, or the United Kingdom, you might not be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF.\n3.  **If you are NOT eligible for VFTF and need to clear immigration (e.g., for baggage or to leave the airport):**\n    *   You will need to apply for a **standard Singapore tourist visa** in advance. Indian citizens cannot get a visa on arrival in Singapore for transit purposes if they don't qualify for VFTF.\n    *   You can apply for this online through authorized visa agents in India. The processing time is typically 3-5 working days, and the fee is around SGD 30 (approx. INR 1,800-2,500).\n\n**To summarize:**\n\n*   **Philippines Visa:** You're good for the Philippines.\n*   **Singapore Layover:**\n    *   If your baggage is checked through, your layover is short, and you stay within the transit area, you likely **don't need a Singapore visa**.\n    *   If you need to clear immigration (for baggage or to leave the airport) and you **do not** have a valid visa from one of the AJACSSUK countries, you **will need a Singapore tourist visa** applied for in advance.\n\nIt's always best to confirm with your airlines regarding baggage transfer and check the official ICA (Immigration & Checkpoints Authority) Singapore website for the most up-to-date transit visa information for Indian Visa Free Transit Facility Back to top The information on this page is a guide for visitors who may qualify for the 96-hour visa free transit facility (VFTF). This may be available to nationals of the Commonwealth of Independent States (CIS) [1] , Georgia, India,\n    Turkmenistan and Ukraine; as well as selected [2] Nationals of the People's Republic of China (PRC) who are not holding ordinary, diplomatic, public affairs\n    or service PRC passports. Transit travellers who satisfy the criteria for VFTF will have to meet the prevailing entry requirements before they are allowed entry into Singapore. Assessment for entry is determined by the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at\n    the point of entry. Extension of stay for transit travellers under VFTF is strictly not allowed. [1] CIS refers to: Armenia, Azerbaijan, Belarus, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Moldova, Russia, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan. [2] These include holder of the PRC Travel Document, Hong Kong Special Administrative Region (SAR) Document of Identity (DOI) and Macau SAR Travel Permit. Holder of ordinary, diplomatic, public affairs and service PRC passports are excluded. Nationals of India and selected [2] Nationals of PRC If you are in transit to or from any third country via Singapore, you may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you meet these requirements: You may enter Singapore by any mode of transport but depart only via air or sea. You must have a valid onward air/ferry/cruise ticket departing Singapore within 96 hours; and You must have a valid visa [3] or long-term pass (with a validity of at least one month from the date of entry into Singapore under the VFTF) issued by and/or good for entry into any of the following countries: - Australia - Canada - Germany - Japan - New Zealand - Switzerland - United Kingdom - United States of America [3] Travellers with Single Journey Visas (SJV) may still be granted VFTF on the return leg of their journey (i.e., after the SJV is used and no longer valid), but you must: Travel directly from the country that issued the SJV, en route through Singapore, back to your home country Not have returned to your home country since you last used the SJV. Nationals of the CIS [1] , Georgia, Turkmenistan and Ukraine If you are in transit to or from any third country, you may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you have a valid onward air ticket departing within 96 hours. You may enter Singapore by any mode of transport but must depart Singapore by air. Click on individual countries or regions below to find out more. Countries Afghanistan , Algeria , Armenia , Azerbaijan , Bangladesh , Belarus , Democratic People's Republic of Korea , Egypt , Georgia , Iran , Iraq , Jordan , Kazakhstan , Kosovo , Kyrgyzstan , Lebanon , Libya , Mali , Moldova , Morocco , Nigeria , Pakistan , Russia , Sudan , South Sudan , Syria , Somalia , Turkmenistan , Tajikistan , Tunisia , Ukraine , Uzbekistan , Yemen You will also need a visa if you are travelling on: A Refugee travel document. This travel document is subject to assessment of recognition for entry into Singapore An Alien's passport. This travel document is subject to assessment of recognition for entry into Singapore A Palestinian Authority passport A temporary passport issued by the United Arab Emirates A Document of Identity issued by Hong Kong Special Administrative Region A Travel Permit issued by Macao Special Administrative Region A PRC Travel Document SUBMISSION OF DOCUMENTS You can apply for your visa through any of the authorised visa agents at Annex A (233.30KB) . Visa applications will be accepted only when all of the following documents are in order: To apply for an entry visa for business or social visits, you will need the following documents: Duly completed Form 14A ( https://www.ica.gov.sg/docs/default-source/ica/forms/form14a.pdf ) signed by you. Valid passport with at least six months validity on the date of entry into Singapore 2 passport-sized colour photograph taken within the last three months. Please see Photo Guidelines for information on photo requirements. Additional supporting documents (e.g., Form V39A * (Letter of Introduction for Visa Application (LOI)) may be required on a case-by-case basis. *The local contact must be either a Singapore citizen (SC) or a Singapore permanent resident (PR) who is at least 21 years old. If you are applying for an entry visa for business visit (attending business negotiations or discussions), the local contact must be acting on behalf of the Singapore-registered business entity. LOIs issued by your Embassy/High Commission may also be accepted, if you are unable to secure an LOI from a local contact to support your visa application for social and business visits. All documents in Hindi, including official notes, should be translated into English language VISA PROCESSING TIME The processing time for visa applications will generally take 3 to 5 working days (excluding date of submission, weekends and public holidays). Some applications may take longer to process. Visa applicants are not required to buy a flight ticket before applying for an entry visa. Applicants who choose to buy a flight ticket will have to bear the risk of loss if the visa application outcome is unsuccessful or if the approved visa duration falls outside of the applicant’s intended date of travel. Submission of documents to Authorised Visa Agent ► Agent to submit documents to High Commission online ►Processing time ► Result of Visa Photo Guidelines Taken within the last 3 months Clear and in focus, 35mm wide by 45mm high, without any borders In colour taken against a plain white background without shadows Avoid white headgear and plain white clothing (headgear worn in accordance with religious or racial customs is acceptable but must not hide any facial features) Full face with the upper shoulders visible, facing forward with eyes open Hair must not cover the eyebrows or the eyes Even exposure (must not be taken under direct lighting) Spectacle frames must not cover part of the eyes Spectacle lenses must not reflect or glare Please see Photo Guidelines for more information on photo requirements. VISA FEES The visa processing fee for each application is INR 1900 . Visa processing fees are paid upon application of visas. The processing fee is non-refundable regardless of the outcome of the application or if you withdraw the application after submission. The visa processing fee of INR 1900 is non-refundable. Our Authorised Visa Agents are permitted to charge a service fee of Rs.1000 on top of the visa processing fee. All fees are paid upon the application of the visa. IMPORTANT NOTICES Incomplete forms and/or documents submitted may result in the application being rejected or the processing delayed. Every application for a visa, including the duration of the visa granted, will be considered on its own merits and subject to approval. Possession of a visa does not guarantee entry into Singapore. The granting of entry into Singapore is determined by the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at the point of entry. Visitors must also meet entry requirements such as holding a valid passport, sufficient funds for the period of stay in Singapore and confirmed onward/return air ticket (if applicable). The validity of the visa refers to the period where the holder may seek entry into Singapore. The duration of stay is determined by ICA officers on duty at the respective checkpoints. A Singapore visa is not an immigration pass. It is a pre-entry permission for the holder of a valid Singapore visa to travel to, and seek entry, into Singapore. The grant of an immigration pass will be determined by the ICA officers at the point of entry. Possession of a valid visa alone does not guarantee entry into Singapore. All foreign visitors arriving in Singapore, with effect from 11 March 2022, will be notified digitally of their Visit Pass (e-Pass) via email. There will not be any endorsement on the passport. The e-Pass notification will indicate the period of stay granted and the last day of stay allowed . Please ensure that you enter a valid email when submitting the SG Arrival Card . CHECKING STATUS OF VISA APPLICATION To check the status of the visa application, please follow the steps below. If you had already been issued with a valid visa but misplaced the copy of your e-Visa, you can also follow the same steps to print a copy of your e-Visa. Step 1: Go to Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) website at www.ica.gov.sg Step 2: At ICA homepage, scroll down to section <e-Services and Forms> and click on <Apply entry visa)> Step 3: Scroll the page down and click on either <Enquire Application Status> or <Enquire Family Application Status> depend on type of application. Step 4: Key in your <Visa Reference Number ( DELXXXXXXSAXXXXXXXX ) > and <Applicant’s Travel Document Number ( XXXXXXXX )> and click <Next> Step 5: If your application is approved, click on <Print e-Visa> to get the copy of the e-Visa Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF) India nationals who are in transit to or from any third country by air may be eligible for 96-hours Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF). An Indian traveller who is not in possession of a valid Singapore visa and not eligible for VFTF, will not be allowed to enter Singapore. The following conditions apply for Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF) General Conditions: A.                  Transit travellers who satisfy the criteria for VFTF will have to meet the prevailing entry requirements before they are allowed entry into Singapore. Assessment for entry is determined by the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at the point of entry. B.                  Extension of stay for transit travellers under the VFTF is strictly not allowed. Travellers who are nationals of India Indian nationals who are in transit to or from any third country via Singapore, you may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you meet these requirements: You may enter Singapore by any mode of transport but depart only via air or sea. You must have a valid onward air/ferry/cruise ticket departing Singapore within 96 hours; and You must have a valid visa*/long-term pass (with a validity of at least 1 month from the date of entry into Singapore under the VFTF) issued by any of the following countries: Australia Canada Germany^ Japan New Zealand Switzerland^ United Kingdom United States of America * A visa is considered valid so long as it is good for entry into one of the eight countries listed above. Travelers with Single Journey Visas (SJV) may still be granted VFTF on the return leg of their journey (i.e. after the SJV is used and no longer valid), but you must: Travel directly from the country that issued the SJV, en route through Singapore, back to your home country Not have returned to your home country since you last used the SJV. ^ Applies to visas issued by Germany or Switzerland, or Schengen visas that are good for entry to Germany or Switzerland. Nationals of the Commonwealth of Independent States (CIS), Georgia, Turkmenistan and Ukraine If you are in transit to or from any third country, you may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you have a valid onward air ticket departing within 96 hours. You may enter Singapore by any mode of transport but must depart Singapore by air. Notice: The High Commission would like to remind members of the public that the High Commission does not process and issue work passes. All applications for work passes have to be submitted by the potential employers in Singapore to the Singapore Ministry of Manpower directly. If you require further clarification or have any doubts, you may contact the Ministry of Manpower at email: mom_feedback@mom.gov.sg . There have been cases of fraudulent work passes being issued in India. You may contact the Accounting and Corporate Regulatory Authority (ACRA) to verify the authenticity of the company registered in Singapore and the Ministry of Manpower for verification of work permits/passes. AI Summary Navigating Singapore transit visas for Indian citizens can be straightforward. Generally, a visa isn't needed if you remain within Changi Airport's transit area. However, venturing outside requires either a standard visa or eligibility for the 96-hour Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF). VFTF: Requires confirmed onward tickets and a valid visa from countries like the US, UK, or Australia. Standard Visa: Apply online via authorized agents. Costs SGD 30 with a 3-5 day processing time. Key Facts (2025): Stricter VFTF checks, online-only standard visa applications, and no visa on arrival. Passport validity must exceed six months. Understanding these requirements ensures a smooth transit experience in Singapore. Put simply, no Indian travellers are exempt from a Singapore transit visa when they stay within Changi Airport’s international transit lounge. However, if you intend to leave the airport for sightseeing or need to transit between terminals that call for immigration clearance, you must either: Be eligible for the 96-hour Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF), or Obtain a standard Singapore visa prior to travelling. The visa requirement during transit depends on whether you remain airside or plan to enter the country. No visa, VFTF still valid Selecting the option to leave the airport outside the VFTF criteria? Visa required Facts About Key Transit Visa to Indian Travellers (By July 2025) Below are the facts about the key transit visa for Indian travellers: VFTF Eligibility Is More Determining: Indian nationals intending to use the Visa-Free Transit Facility are now required to show confirmed onward tickets and a valid 3rd country visa ( e.g. to the US or UK, Canada, Australia, etc.). Immigration officers may permit or deny entry under VFTF and therefore cannot be assured beforehand. Only on the Internet (Standard Visa): Unless you are a VFTF eligible visitor and wish to leave the airport, you will have to use a regular Singapore visa, which can be obtained only online, either by using the Immigration and Checkpoints Authority (ICA) webpage or at a recruited visa agent. There are no arrival visits. Visa not required to transit the airside: As long as you remain within the restricted transit area (airside) of Changi Airport and you do not clear immigration, you do not need a visa or VFTF, whether you are of Singapore nationality or not, or your layover time is simply a few hours or more. This will only be applicable in case the connecting flight is on the same reservation or airline system. Who Qualifies for the 96-Hour Free Transit (VFTF)? To qualify under the 96-hour Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF) by Singapore, Indian passport holders are required to satisfy all the following conditions, and it is worthwhile to note the caveats as well: 1. Confirmation of Onward Travel in 96 hours A valid flight ticket out of Singapore up to 96 hours before arrival should be provided by the traveller. The one in question should remain a received ticket, which is not open-ended, but onward travel to a third country is clearly indicated. Travels by land or sea (e.g. to Malaysia) are not acceptable under VFTF, unless it is approved under exceptional cases. 2. Legal Visa or Residence Permit of Particular Countries Should possess a legal visa (single-entry or multiple-entry) or a long-term residence permit granted by the: Australia Canada Japan New Zealand Switzerland United Kingdom Germany United States The traveler is only required to have a single entry visa which has been utilized already in the event that in case he or she is returning directly to the country. 3. Travelling Direction is Important You have to be on a journey to or passing through the country that issues visas (given above). For example: India (USA (Valid)) [As of March 2008] USA = Singapore = India (Also valid) India->Singapore->Thailand (It is not allowed when utilising VFTF, unless returning to a country that issued the Visa) 4. Passport Validity The passport should be valid for 6 months after the date of arrival in Singapore. Damaged or fake passports can also lead to rejection of travel even when other prerequisites have been fulfilled. 5. Evidence of Good Faith Money The officials might also be requested to produce evidence of financial status to sustain the stay and further travel to the country. This may consist of credit/debit card, cash or bank statements. This can be assessed fully at the discretion of the immigration officers. 6. No History of Immigrant Offences A traveller can be disqualified due to previous violations or overstaying in Singapore or other nations. The better travelling record gives weight and minimises the possibility of rejection. You have a layover that exceeds 96 hours. You must collect your baggage or move between terminals outside the transit area. How to Apply Online Singapore Transit Visa for Indians: Step-by-Step Process 2025 The procedure of obtaining a Singapore visa online is as follows: 1. Prepare Required Documents: Indian passport (minimum 6 months validity) Completed Singapore visa application Form 14A Recent passport-sized photographs as per specifications Confirmed onward and return flight tickets Bank statement (minimum INR 50,000, recent) Cover letter explaining travel purpose (on company letterhead if self-employed) Other supporting documents as required by agents. 2. Apply Through Authorized Visa Agents: Direct applications to Singapore missions in India are generally not accepted. Submit application via authorised travel/visa agents. 3. Pay Visa Fees: Standard visa fee is SGD 30 (~INR 1,800-2,500) non-refundable. 4. Track Application Status Online: Use the details given by agents or the ICA online portal to track progress. 5. Receive E-Visa and Print: Upon approval, print the e-visa to carry during travel. Confirmation ticket for the onward flight. Accommodation proof (where applicable). Bank statement/ proof of funds. Other supporting documents, such as a third-country visa (unless ineligible VFTF). Useful Links Categories Canada Germany United Kingdom Miscellaneous Australia Frequently Asked Questions How do I know the status of my Singapore transit visa application? You can check the status of your Singapore transit visa application through the authorized visa agent you submitted your application with, as they usually provide updates and tracking facilities. If you applied online and received a unique application reference number, you can also track the status on the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) official website by entering your passport number, application reference, and date of birth. Typically, the visa processing time takes around 3 to 5 working days, excluding weekends and public holidays, so checking with your agent or through ICA’s portal will give you the latest update on your application. Is the Singapore transit visa your transit visa? Yes, Singapore does not have a separate transit visa category. Instead, the standard Singapore tourist visa is used when you need to transit through Singapore and want to leave the airport or stay temporarily in the city. If you are merely changing flights without clearing immigration and you qualify for the Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF), you may not require any visa at all. However, in cases where you plan to enter Singapore even for a short duration during transit, holding a valid Singapore tourist visa acts as your transit visa. But what would happen to Indian travellers in case of an overstay of a transit visa? Overstaying a Singapore transit or tourist visa can lead to serious consequences. Indian travelers who overstay may be subjected to monetary fines based on the length of the overstay. In severe cases, authorities may detain or deport the individual, and the overstay can lead to blacklisting, where future visa applications to Singapore might be denied. It is essential to comply with the visa validity period and leave Singapore on or before the expiry date to avoid these penalties and complications. Is there any way my Singapore transit visa can be extended? No, Singapore does not allow extensions on short-term transit or tourist visas, including the Visa-Free Transit Facility. Travelers must exit Singapore within the visa or transit facility validity period granted at entry. Extensions are generally not granted except under extreme situations such as serious medical emergencies, which must be supported by valid official documentation. In normal circumstances, you should plan your travel to avoid overstaying. When flying into one Singapore airport and out of another do I require an additional transit visa? Singapore has only one major international airport, Changi Airport, which consists of multiple terminals (Terminal 1, 2, 3, and 4 along with Jewel Changi). Since all terminals are part of the same airport complex, you do not require an additional transit visa if you are transferring between terminals within Changi. However, if you intend to exit the airport, you need to have a valid visa or qualify for the Visa-Free Transit Facility. There is no situation where you would need an additional transit visa for flying in and out of different Singaporean airports because no other international airport operates in Singapore. See all posts Frank Gogol Singapore is a popular destination for travelers from all over the world. As a major transportation hub in Southeast Asia, many travelers transit through Singapore on their way to other destinations. Indian citizens are no exception to this, and many of them transit through Singapore on their way to other countries. However, Indian citizens who are transiting through Singapore need to be aware of the eligibility criteria and application process for the Singapore transit visa. Read on to learn everything you need to know! Key Takeaways Indian citizens transiting Singapore need a passport valid for 6 months, adhere to Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF) rules for air transit, and provide documents like onward ticket, valid visa for destination, and proof of funds. Apply online via ICA website, pay SGD 30 fee, provide personal and travel details, and necessary documents. Visa is single entry, valid for 14 days, and non-refundable. Allows 96-hour stay, single entry within 14 days, not for multiple entries or tourism/business. Entry subject to ICA officer approval. Eligibility Criteria for Singapore Transit Visa for Indians To transit through Singapore, Indian citizens must meet certain eligibility criteria and entry requirements. This section will outline the key requirements for obtaining a Singapore transit visa. Passport Validity Requirements Indian citizens must have a valid passport with at least six months’ validity beyond the date of entry into Singapore. If the passport is due to expire within six months, the traveler must renew their passport before applying for a transit visa. Transit Visa Exemptions Indian citizens may be eligible for a Visa Free Transit Facility (VFTF) if they are transiting through Singapore to a third country by air. The VFTF allows Indian citizens to transit visa-free by air for up to 96 hours. However, to be eligible for the VFTF, Indian citizens must meet additional eligibility criteria and entry requirements. Necessary Supporting Documents To apply for a Singapore transit visa, Indian citizens must provide the following documents: Valid passport with at least six months’ validity beyond the date of entry into Singapore Confirmed onward air ticket to a third country within 96 hours of arrival in Singapore Valid visa or long-term pass for the third country of destination (if required) Sufficient funds for the duration of stay in Singapore In addition, Indian citizens must also satisfy the immigration officer at the point of entry that they meet the entry requirements for Singapore. It is recommended that travelers carry copies of all necessary documents to present to immigration officers upon request. Overall, Indian citizens who meet the eligibility criteria and entry requirements can transit through Singapore with ease. It is important to note that the requirements for obtaining a Singapore transit visa are subject to change, so travelers should always check the latest information before planning their trip. Application Process for Singapore Transit Visa If you are an Indian traveler planning to transit through Singapore, you need to obtain a transit visa before your trip. Here is a brief guide on how to apply for a Singapore Transit Visa. Online Application Procedure The application for a Singapore Transit Visa can be submitted online through the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) website. Here are the steps to follow: Go to the ICA website and click on “e-Service” under the “Visa” section. Select “Transit Visa” and then choose “India” as your country of citizenship. Fill in the required personal and travel information, including your flight details and the purpose of your visit. Upload the necessary supporting documents, such as your passport copy, photograph, and flight itinerary. Review and submit your application. Once your application is submitted, you will receive an application reference number that you can use to track the status of your application. Required Fees and Payment Methods The fee for a Singapore Transit Visa is SGD 30, which is equivalent to approximately INR 1,600. The fee can be paid online using a credit card or through an eNETS account. It is important to note that the visa fee is non-refundable, even if your application is rejected. Therefore, make sure to double-check all the information before submitting your application. In conclusion, obtaining a Singapore Transit Visa for Indians is a straightforward process that can be completed online. Make sure to apply well in advance of your travel date and follow all the instructions carefully to ensure a smooth transit experience. Transit Visa Conditions and Restrictions Maximum Stay Duration Indian travelers who are eligible for the Transit Visa can stay in Singapore for a maximum of 96 hours (4 days). This duration starts from the time of arrival in Singapore. If the traveler intends to stay in Singapore for more than 96 hours, they will need to apply for a regular visa. Visa Validity Period The Transit Visa is valid for a single entry only. It is important to note that the visa must be used within 14 days from the date of issuance. The validity period of the visa is not the same as the duration of stay. The validity period refers to the time during which the visa can be used to enter Singapore. Conditions for Multiple Entries The Transit Visa is not valid for multiple entries. Travelers who need to enter Singapore multiple times will need to apply for a regular visa. Additionally, the Transit Visa is only valid for travelers who are transiting through Singapore on their way to a third country. It cannot be used for travelers who are entering Singapore for the purpose of tourism or business. It is important to note that the Transit Visa does not guarantee entry into Singapore. The final decision on entry into Singapore lies with the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officer at the point of entry. Travelers must ensure that they meet all the requirements for entry into Singapore, including having a valid passport, sufficient funds for the duration of their stay, and a return ticket to their country of origin or onward destination. Overall, the Transit Visa is a convenient option for Indian travelers who are transiting through Singapore on their way to a third country. By understanding the conditions and restrictions of the Transit Visa, travelers can ensure a smooth and hassle-free transit experience. Singapore is a major stopover point for international flights, thanks to its great location and the famous Changi Airport. If you're an Indian citizen flying through Singapore, it’s important to understand the visa rules to avoid any travel hiccups. This guide explains what you need to know about the Singapore transit visa. Suppose travellers plan to explore Singapore but do not meet the transit requirements. In that case, they can easily apply for a Singapore tourist visa through Atlys , a fast, reliable, and hassle-free visa application platform. What is a transit visa for Singapore? A transit visa lets you pass through Singapore to another country. Whether you need one depends on your nationality and travel plans. For some people, Singapore offers a Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF) , which means they can enter the country for a short time, without a visa, if they meet certain conditions. Do Indian citizens need a transit visa for Singapore? No. You might not need one if you only pass through on your way to another country. You can stay in the transit area at Changi airport, or if you qualify for the VFTF , which allows up to 96 hours of visa-free stay in Singapore. To use the VFTF, you must meet a few specific conditions, such as having a valid visa for your next destination. Remember that even if you meet all the rules, immigration officers at the airport make the final decision. Changi Airport has three main terminals- Terminal 1, Terminal 2, and Terminal 3, all seamlessly connected by a Skytrain system. Travellers can move between these terminals without clearing immigration , as the Skytrain operates entirely within the transit (airside) area. Therefore, no transit visa is required to transfer between terminals or to use the Skytrain, provided you remain inside the transit zone. What are the eligibility criteria of VFTF Singapore? Indian citizens transiting through Singapore may be eligible for the 96-hour Visa-Free Transit Facility if the following conditions are met: The traveller is in transit to or from a third country (not returning directly to India). Entry into Singapore may be by any mode of transport, but departure must be by air or sea within 96 hours. The traveller must hold a valid visa or long-term pass (valid for at least one month from the date of entry) issued by, or valid for entry into, Australia, Canada, Germany, Japan, New Zealand, Switzerland, the United Kingdom, or the United States. Please note: Travellers with valid visas or passes from any countries listed above can enter and explore Singapore for up to 96 hours under the VFTF. No additional Singapore visa is required, provided all other VFTF eligibility criteria are met. Single Journey Visa (SJV) holders may use VFTF only on the return leg of their journey, directly from the visa-issuing country to India via Singapore, without re-entering India before transiting. Please note: Entry under VFTF is not guaranteed and is subject to the assessment of Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at the point of entry. Extension of stay is not permitted under the VFTF. What if I don’t meet the requirements and have to transit through Singapore? If you have a connecting flight and your baggage is checked through, you can remain in the transit area at Changi Airport. In this case, you do not need a visa or VFTF, since you are not entering Singapore. What is the Singapore transit visa processing time for Indians? No processing time is involved for travellers eligible under the VFTF, as no prior application is required. Entry is assessed upon arrival. What is the Singapore transit visa cost for Indians? The VFTF is not a visa and does not require any application fee or prior approval, but entry is still subject to approval by Singapore’s Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) What can you expect when you are in the Singapore transit immigration process? Indian travellers transiting through Singapore, especially under the Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF), must undergo standard immigration clearance. Below are the key steps and requirements: All travellers must undergo immigration checks at their entry point (airport, seaport, or land checkpoint). You may use the automated clearance lanes, especially at Changi Airport, if eligible. Otherwise, proceed to the manual immigration counters. During clearance, you must present your biometrics (fingerprints, facial, and/or iris scans). Failure to comply may lead to denial of entry. Be prepared to present the following during transit immigration checks: Valid passport (at least 6 months validity) Confirmed onward travel tickets (within 96 hours, if using VFTF) Valid visa or long-term pass for the next country, if applicable (especially under VFTF) Proof of sufficient funds for your stay in Singapore Transiting through Singapore offers Indian travellers an opportunity to experience the city's offerings, even if briefly. The Visa-Free Transit Facility simplifies this process, eliminating the need for a formal visa application. By understanding and meeting the eligibility criteria, Indian nationals can ensure a seamless transit experience through Singapore. Do Indian citizens need a transit visa for Singapore? Not always. No visa is needed if you remain in the transit area at Changi Airport during a layover. If you wish to enter Singapore and meet the eligibility requirements, you can use the Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF) for up to 96 hours without a visa. What documents do I need to use the VFTF? You must carry: A passport valid for at least 6 months, Confirmed onward air or sea ticket (within 96 hours), A valid visa or long-term pass for one of the eight eligible countries, Proof of funds (recommended), And comply with biometric procedures at immigration. Singapore VFTF transit visa rules Singapore VTFT is valid only for 96 hours or 4 days. This is a single entry visa. You can use it only once during your onward or return journey. You can wait in the Singapore Airport’s Transit area until your connecting flight arrives. Extension of Singapore VFTF is strictly prohibited. Staying in Singapore after the expiry of your VFTF is punishable by law. VFTF is not issued by the Singapore Embassy or Singapore High Commissions in India. Perhaps you’re connecting to another destination, or maybe even taking a short break during your travels.  This post will completely clarify the transit visa rules for Indian passport holders, saving you time, money, and potential airport headaches. We cover everything you need to know to ensure a smooth and stress-free transit through Singapore’s Changi Airport. Understanding Singapore’s Transit Visa Rules for Indians Let’s cut to the chase: many Indian nationals can transit through Singapore without a visa, but the rules depend on several factors. Who needs a transit visa? Generally, you’ll need to apply for a Singapore transit visa if you plan to leave the transit area of the airport during your layover. That said this wouldn’t be many people with a short stop over to switch airlines & connecting flights. If you intend to explore Singapore during any part of your layover it’s crucial that you obtain this visa. Also this is a necessary action if you airline has booked separate baggage checks etc meaning you aren’t transitting on a connected single airline. Who is exempt from needing a transit visa? Many Indian citizens are exempt if they meet specific conditions— mostly the duration of their stopover within Singapore and the types of transit. In short; this is all primarily defined by the very handy “96-Hour Visa Free Transit Programme”. Transit without a Visa: The 96-Hour Transit Programme This incredibly beneficial programme allows eligible Indian citizens to transit through Singapore’s Changi Airport without requiring a transit visa provided their layover doesn’t exceed 96 hours, or indeed in cases with a layover exceeding this limit, if their luggage is directly checked onto  their flight of origin to their target flights end destinion and their airline documents verify this action before boarding in all circumstances. Eligibility requirements generally specify: Confirmation you plan not to exit Singapore’ destination and will transition to the connected flight with all checked baggage directly onward from when boarded on the flight of origin. Your passport’s validity is 6 more than months  from the travel start date. You have a confirmed onward flight ticket as you arrive. Applying for this transit scheme:  There aren’t individual registration rules to ensure your inclusion but your airline tickets must be compliant with all the criteria for it’s applicability. What if my layover is less than 96 hours? If the conditions set out in section 2 and 3 to include this Transit Agreement are all present you will be provided transit documentation by your airline company. If that it however isn’t the case you don’t usually need to actively apply for anything, especially if you do indeed stay restricted to Changi Airport’’s transit facilities and don’t exit any airport areas during the entire duration of your stopover. You must however carry essential documents that usually, the airline will assist with obtaining for compliant situations: Your passport (again validity extending 6+ months from travel start). Onward/Return air tickets (with confirmed bookings). Any visa, or documentation clarifying this may well not in fact be applicable if appropriate Transit scheme are successfully confirmed. With a shorter connection a big thing to remember isn’t about visas; you’re mostly concerning yourself with timings. Especially important to ensure you manage timings during a shortlayover: check in, customs screening, transfer between terminals etc. and keep track of flight time changes (be realistic around such times, always build contingency in planning any journey). Many successful flights may be delayed sometimes. Be appropriately prepared, informed and plan. Also be familiar where the gates of entry are from your destination airport’ terminal. What to do if you need a Transit Visa Should you’ve come to finding you’re not eligible from any of sections 2 & section 3 then; regrettably  you will need to apply formally for the required documents as part of your Transit. Contact directly to your own nearest Singaporean embassy & relevant authorities. This will almost certainly apply to extended duration transit stopovers & cases where you in fact do wish to engage exploring Singapore at any juncture of your travel (or wish to claim excess baggae as some baggage transfer conditions can need sorting within the Airport transit facilities). The detailed process and precise forms for visas may vary so verify all appropriate guidance before initiating application action, but your application will involve all the usual passport, confirmation of travel times documents etc and all other specifics of your travel route before your requested departure date on connecting services. Common Mistakes to Avoid Some aspects often found overlooked can prevent smoothly entering Singapore are Underestimating Transit visa applicability rules : Review all information from your flights on both entry arrival & outbound connections. Doublecheck airline terms  before the flight to avoid last-minute surprises. Know if your connection involves new baggage, and are flights between common connections with one airline handling multiple flight legs for onward connection (including your luggage etc and other travel arrangements directly). Inadequate Passport Duration :  Ensure your passport retains several additional months beyond your all journey’s transit or stop over planned date end points. A limited passport duration frequently creates rejection of immigration procedures as a common standard immigration check and that creates significant hindrance. Missing or Insufficient support Documentation : Not taking comprehensive paperwork, that verifies airline journey plans  etc which supports any necessary baggage arrangements, confirmed flights etc including necessary visa aspects (if required) can cause disruption . Keeping copies and other appropriate documents to  address  all your arrangements may ensure there can be smooth actions. Ensure it isn’t lost. Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs) What if my connecting flight is delayed? Delays happen– this may delay your transfer on many transit plans and this would still affect on-transit aspects in particular in most cases. Therefore keeping contact with both destination Airport transit officials throughout, in addition any relevant Airport’ connections will enable any necessary guidance as this occurs. Many helpful officials with useful advice are commonly available for immediate guidance. Plan alternative plans well in advance! Plan as early as possible to ensure alternative possibilities exist around travel to ensure the possibility of making your transit. For instance your accommodation has to be organised much in advance since most hotels have a standard set of cancellations policies. Plan appropriately to any delay conditions by verifying any hotel stay insurance etc to minimise any travel difficulties. Can I leave the airport during my transit? Only if you conform with Singaporean visa requirements for permitted visits during relevant transit arrangements. Check any applicable Transit Agreement details such as Singapore’s 96-hour plan and so verify compliance before leaving Airport facilities. Failing to conform with procedures  might involve consequences you may well be responsible for incurring. Ensure also for many airlines and other transfers the timings may also have a bearing too–  plan adequately with this factored accordingly as much or more is important. What happens if I don’t meet the transit visa requirements? you won’t normally be granted clearance to depart your inward journey. Any action of continuing travel if you are deemed having non compliant procedures for transit etc will almost inevitably require reappraisals often creating added complexities & significant delays before proceeding again your next leg of any trans-journey. Where can I find more information about Singapore visa requirements? Check the official Singaporean Immigration & Checkpoints Authorities website, or seek advice from embassies of relevant Singapore Embassies appropriately or other similar international governing entities that should apply (including also airline travel agent sources to determine appropriate validity). Direct contact to official sources is always superior for getting advice when dealing with documentation validation since this requires expert verification. Often the correct authority determines any special requirements. Can I apply for a transit visa on arrival? Unfortunately, for this particular travel plan no direct “on arrival” visas are granted; you would’d almost always needing completing necessary applications prior to travelling, with often long processing periods with much bureaucratic administration that is normally in place to manage this documentation. Conclusion Successfully transiting through Singapore from India critically involves comprehending those sometimes nuanced transit visa rules that apply to specifically for Indian nationals such as that offered via “The 96-hour Visa programme” etc. This requires carefully verifying your exact travel details including all luggage arrangements and timing of journeys as required by compliance standards for official procedures – by taking note from early stages what actions might apply according to those transit routes you plan enables very many successful transit opportunities through the wonderful efficient service available through Singapore Airport transport and connecting providers.  Planning appropriate travel strategy with appropriate flexibility to permit unforeseen disruptions helps mitigate risk and minimise possibilities unexpected hold-ups and potential disruptions as much as possible. Verifying passport duration before journeys creates often greatly successful, smooth transfer throughout  without incurring many or indeed all possible  administrative processing delays; thereby offering far more efficient transit for your onward  travel plans (very common among numerous experienced seasoned travellers).  Many successfully achieve excellent travel via planning – share experience via discussions; this blog-based knowledge sharing through open discourse generates mutual help for the benefit providing better guidance for all readers. Visa-Free Transit Facility Also Known as VFTF Singapore How to Apply for a Singapore Transit Visa? What is Singapore Transit Visa Processing Time? What are the Fees for Singapore Transit Visa? Need Assistance with your Singapore Layover Visa? If you are an Indian traveler flying through Singapore, you may think, Do Indians need a transit visa for Singapore? Well, the answer relies on your layover duration, travel plan, and, yes, the final destination. This blog guide describes everything you need to learn about the Singapore transit visa while knowing when the visa is required, how to apply, and the other Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF). Is There a Need for Singapore Transit Visa for Indians Well, Indian passport holders do not usually need a transit visa for Singapore. They can transit without a visa if: You stay in the transit area of the airport. The layover is less than 24 hours. You are eligible for the Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF). Nevertheless, if you intend to exit the airport or have different flight tickets with other airlines, you might require a Singapore transit visa. What Documents are Required for Singapore Transit Visa? A passport with a minimum validity of 6 months Visa Application Form 14A Minimum of three passport-sized photographs A cover letter with all the travel details Confirmed flight tickets and also the hotel bookings (if any) Also Want to Know About Singapore Arrival Card (SGAC) Check Now! When do Indian travelers not require a Singapore Transit Visa? Indian passport holders can travel through Singapore without a need for a transit visa in these circumstances: Short Layovers in the Transit Area: Transit Without Visa (TWOV) Eligibility: Traveling to the UK, USA, Australia, Canada, or New Zealand with a valid visa. Single-Ticket Connections: If luggage is checked in and no immigration is needed. Airline-Specific Agreements: Some airlines permit easy transits without visa clearance. Exploring the Changi Airport Without Immigration: Enjoy theaters, movies, gardens, and lounges, all inside the transit zone. Visa-Free Transit Facility Also Known as VFTF Singapore VFTF Singapore is a 96-hour Visa Free Transit Facility (Singapore VFTF). It permits Indian passport holders to move through Singapore for up to 96 hours, which is 4 days, without requiring a transit visa. It is kind of a Singapore layover visa. To prepare for VFTF Singapore, Indian travelers must have either a valid visa or long-term residence permit from Canada, Australia, the USA, the UK, New Zealand, Germany, or Switzerland. You have to either be in transit to or from one of these countries and give an onward flight ticket that is confirmed to depart within the tenure of 96 hours to use VFTF Singapore. VFTF Singapore is usable only once per journey. It can be either during your exit or entry, but not both. Travelers using VFTF Singapore are not permitted to overstay. If they do so, it can lead to penalties or even future visa restrictions. VFTF Singapore entry is given at the discretion of the immigration authority, even if all eligibility standards are met. VFTF Singapore enables travelers to scour the city, live in hotels, or just enjoy Changi Airport's world-class luxuries during long layovers. Also Want to Know About OCI Application Singapore Check Now! How to Apply for a Singapore Transit Visa? Below are the detailed steps: Check if you need a transit visa: If you did not meet the criteria for Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF), then you will need a transit visa. Submit the form: You can submit the application online either through an authorised visa agent or you need to go to the  Singapore’s Immigration and Checkpoints Authority (ICA). As an Indian citizen you cannot initiate an application process directly through the ICS portal, you need to have an authorized agent or have to use a Singapore overseas mission. Gather important documents: You need to have these important documents ready like at least a six month validity passport, passport size pictures that meet the visa photo specification standards for singapore, a complete application form, payment proof, onward booked flight tickets from singapore, and proof of sufficient funds. Submit: Once you have gathered all the important documents, submit those along with the application form to either the  Singapore embassy/consulate or your authorized agent. Track your visa: Through the  ICA’s e-Service tracking system, you can easily track your visa status online. Get the visa: Once the application and documents are reviewed by the authorities you will get your visa either by electronic medium or through your agent. What is Singapore Transit Visa Processing Time? Singapore transit visa processing time is usually three to five working days. However, uncertainties like delays in the processing time may occur during peak travel seasons. It is best to apply two weeks before your journey for the Singapore transit visa. What are the Fees for Singapore Transit Visa? The general Singapore Transit Visa Fees is around ₹1,900 approx., although this may change if any additional charges are there. This fee is similar to what is charged for a short-term Singapore visa for tourism or transit purposes. Need Assistance with your Singapore Layover Visa? If you are unsure about whether you will qualify for the VFTF Singapore or not and need help with your Singapore layover visa, our team of experts is here to guide you through the process and handle all your Singapore transit Visa requirements , so you can have a stress-free trip. Apply today with Visament , your reliable partner for fast visa processing. That will cover your entry into the Philippines. However, since you have a layover in Singapore, you need to consider Singapore's transit visa requirements as an Indian citizen.\n\nHere's what you need to know:\n\n**Singapore Transit Visa for Indian Citizens**\n\nGenerally, Indian citizens do **not** need a transit visa for Singapore if they meet certain conditions, primarily related to the **Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF)**.\n\n**1. Visa-Free Transit Facility (VFTF) - Up to 96 Hours:**\n\nYou may be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF if you meet these requirements:\n\n*   **Transit to/from a third country:** You must be in transit to or from a third country (meaning, Singapore is not your final destination, and you are not returning directly to India from Singapore after using the VFTF).\n*   **Onward/Return Ticket:** You must have a confirmed onward air or sea ticket departing Singapore within 96 hours.\n*   **Valid Visa/Long-Term Pass from Specific Countries:** You must hold a valid visa or long-term pass (with at least one month's validity from the date of entry into Singapore under the VFTF) issued by one of the following countries:\n    *   Australia\n    *   Canada\n    *   Germany\n    *   Japan\n    *   New Zealand\n    *   Switzerland\n    *   United Kingdom\n    *   United States of America\n*   **Passport Validity:** Your Indian passport must be valid for at least six months beyond your date of arrival in Singapore.\n*   **Sufficient Funds:** You should have sufficient funds to cover your expenses during your stay in Singapore.\n*   **Clean Immigration Record:** You should not have any previous immigration offenses in Singapore or other countries.\n\n**Important Notes on VFTF:**\n\n*   **Entry is at the discretion of immigration officers:** Even if you meet all the criteria, the Immigration & Checkpoints Authority (ICA) officers at the point of entry have the final say on your admission.\n*   **One-time use:** The VFTF is generally valid for only one leg of your journey (either onward or return, but not both).\n*   **No extension:** Extension of stay under VFTF is strictly not allowed.\n*   **Airside Transit:** If your layover is short and you remain within the international transit area of Changi Airport without clearing immigration, you generally do not need any visa, regardless of VFTF eligibility. This is common if your connecting flight is on the same reservation or airline system and your baggage is checked through to your final destination (Philippines).\n\n**2. When You Might Need a Standard Singapore Visa:**\n\nYou would need to apply for a standard Singapore visa if:\n\n*   You do not meet the VFTF requirements (e.g., you don't have a valid visa from one of the listed countries, or your layover exceeds 96 hours).\n*   You intend to leave the airport for sightseeing or need to transit between terminals that require you to clear immigration, and you are not eligible for VFTF.\n*   Your flights are on separate tickets, and you need to collect and re-check your baggage, which would require you to clear immigration.\n\n**In your specific situation:**\n\nYou mentioned you have a flight from Singapore to the Philippines with Star Airline.\n\n*   **Check your layover duration:** How long is your layover in Singapore?\n*   **Check your baggage situation:** Is your baggage checked through directly from India to the Philippines, or do you need to collect it in Singapore and re-check it for your Star Airline flight?\n*   **Check if you intend to leave the airport:** Do you plan to exit the airport and explore Singapore during your layover?\n\n**Recommendations:**\n\n1.  **Confirm Baggage Policy with Air India and Star Airline:**\n    *   Contact Air India to confirm if your baggage will be checked through all the way to the Philippines. If it is, and your layover is short, you might not need to clear immigration in Singapore.\n    *   If your baggage is not checked through, you will likely need to clear immigration in Singapore to collect and re-check it.\n2.  **Assess VFTF Eligibility:**\n    *   Since you have a Philippines visa, that doesn't count as a visa from one of the \"AJACSSUK\" countries for Singapore's VFTF.\n    *   Therefore, unless you also hold a valid visa or long-term pass from Australia, Japan, America (USA), Canada, Schengen states, Singapore, or the United Kingdom, you might not be eligible for the 96-hour VFTF.\n3.  **If you are NOT eligible for VFTF and need to clear immigration (e.g., for baggage or to leave the airport):**\n    *   You will need to apply for a **standard Singapore tourist visa** in advance. Indian citizens cannot get a visa on arrival in Singapore for transit purposes if they don't qualify for VFTF.\n    *   You can apply for this online through authorized visa agents in India. The processing time is typically 3-5 working days, and the fee is around SGD 30 (approx. INR 1,800-2,500).\n\n**To summarize:**\n\n*   **Philippines Visa:** You're good for the Philippines.\n*   **Singapore Layover:**\n    *   If your baggage is checked through, your layover is short, and you stay within the transit area, you likely **don't need a Singapore visa**.\n    *   If you need to clear immigration (for baggage or to leave the airport) and you **do not** have a valid visa from one of the AJACSSUK countries, you **will need a Singapore tourist visa** applied for in advance.\n\nIt's always best to confirm with your airlines regarding baggage transfer and check the official ICA (Immigration & Checkpoints Authority) Singapore website for the most up-to-date transit visa information for Indian citizens."}],"allow_markdown":true,"media_url":null}]},{"id":"05850c14-8e6e-44b8-8fa6-2b1b5b73969c","flow_id":"bf8af7d1-0a61-468d-a1ee-589acea00542","timestamp":"2025-09-21 07:17:53 UTC","sender":"Machine","sender_name":"AI","session_id":"cfd24410-ec2a-4181-a05c-1a4b71532f2c","text":"[\"How long is my layover in Singapore?\",\"Will I need to collect and re‑check baggage there?\",\"Do I meet the Visa‑Free Transit Facility criteria?\",\"What documents are required for a Singapore tourist visa?\"]","files":"[]","edit":false,"duration":null,"properties":{"text_color":null,"background_color":null,"edited":false,"source":{"id":"GroqModel-OiwmL","display_name":"Groq","source":"openai/gpt-oss-120b"},"icon":"Groq","allow_markdown":false,"positive_feedback":null,"state":"partial","targets":[]},"category":"message","content_blocks":[]},{"id":"ead3d750-655f-4400-8c78-768f4d1aa46a","flow_id":"bf8af7d1-0a61-468d-a1ee-589acea00542","timestamp":"2025-09-21 07:22:15 UTC","sender":"User","sender_name":"User","session_id":"cfd24410-ec2a-4181-a05c-1a4b71532f2c","text":"Check your layover duration: How long is your layover in Singapore? Gather Required Documents:**\n    *   **Visa Application Form (Form 14A):** Duly completed and signed.\n    *   **Passport:** Original passport valid for at least six months from your date of entry into Singapore, with at least two blank pages.\n    *   **Photographs:** Two recent passport-sized color photographs (35mm x 45mm), white background, matte finish, 80% face coverage, taken within the last three months.\n    *   **Confirmed Flight Itinerary:** Round-trip flight tickets showing your entry and exit from Singapore.\n    *   **Confirmed Hotel Booking (if applicable):** While you're transiting, some agents might still ask for a dummy booking or a letter stating your transit purpose.\n    *   **Covering Letter:** A letter from you stating the purpose of your visit (transit to the Philippines), your travel dates, and that you will be self-transiting.\n    *   **Financial Proof:** Bank statements for the last 3-6 months, salary slips, or income tax returns to show sufficient funds.\n    *   **Proof of Occupation:**\n        *   **Employed:** Letter from employer, salary slips.\n        *   **Self-employed:** Business registration, company bank statements.\n        *   **Student:** Student ID, bonafide certificate.\n    *   **Philippines Visa Copy:** A copy of your valid Philippines visa.\n\n**3. Submit Application:**\n    *   Submit all documents to your chosen authorized visa agent. Processing Time and Fees:**\n    *   **Processing Time:** Typically 3 to 5 working days. It's advisable to apply well in advance, at least 2-3 weeks before your travel date, to avoid any last-minute issues.\n    *   **Visa Fee:** The official visa fee is SGD 30 (approximately INR 1,800 - 2,500, depending on the exchange rate). The authorized agent will charge an additional service fee on top of this.\n\n**Important Advice:**\n\n*   **Apply ASAP:** Since you have a definite travel plan, start your visa application process immediately.\n*   **Double-check with the Agent:** When you contact an authorized agent, clearly explain your transit situation (self-transit, need to clear immigration, 8-hour layover, onward Philippines visa) to ensure they provide the most accurate document list and guidance.\n\nWithout a valid Singapore visa, you will likely be denied boarding for your flight to Singapore from India, or denied entry upon arrival in Singapore, as you cannot complete your self-transit without clearing immigration."}],"allow_markdown":true,"media_url":null}]},{"id":"e1a3d887-ecf6-4b54-b4b8-1356cce1ebf3","flow_id":"bf8af7d1-0a61-468d-a1ee-589acea00542","timestamp":"2025-09-21 07:22:23 UTC","sender":"Machine","sender_name":"AI","session_id":"cfd24410-ec2a-4181-a05c-1a4b71532f2c","text":"[\"Do you already have a Singapore tourist visa?\",\"Which authorized visa agent will you use?\",\"Do you have proof of onward travel to the Philippines?\",\"Do you need a dummy hotel booking for the transit?\"]","files":"[]","edit":false,"duration":null,"properties":{"text_color":null,"background_color":null,"edited":false,"source":{"id":"GroqModel-OiwmL","display_name":"Groq","source":"openai/gpt-oss-120b"},"icon":"Groq","allow_markdown":false,"positive_feedback":null,"state":"partial","targets":[]},"category":"message","content_blocks":[]},{"id":"de0f168c-525e-4194-8fce-46968d78784b","flow_id":"bf8af7d1-0a61-468d-a1ee-589acea00542","timestamp":"2025-09-30 19:33:15 UTC","sender":"User","sender_name":"User","session_id":"b7aca738-8054-46a9-8218-3b2507849c9c","text":"I am India citizen, looking to travel to United States for a purpose of Business.
